Common Diseases and Disorders

PAGE 752 · 第 752 页 Chapter Opener & Learning Objectives / 开篇与学习目标

CHAPTER 33: Common Diseases and Disorders. Comprehensive support work requires a solid grounding in the pathophysiology, clinical presentations, and empathetic client-centred care for prevalent acute and chronic medical conditions.

第33章：常见疾病与失调。高质量的支持服务必须牢固建立在对常见急慢性临床病症的病理生理机制、典型临床表现以及以服务对象为中心的同理心照护准则的基础之上。

CHAPTER 33: Common Diseases and Disorders

第 33 章：常见疾病与失调

LEARNING OBJECTIVES / 本章官方教学核心目标

After reading this chapter, the learner should be able to do the following: / 完成本章研读后，学习者应能达到并胜任以下各项专业护理知识与执业能力指标：

  • • Describe the common communicable diseases and the care required.
    详述常见传染病（如流感、病毒性肝炎、STIs、新冠病毒等）的传播机制、隔离预防与支持工作者照护规范。
  • • Describe the common types of cancers and some of the treatments available.
    详述常见癌症类型（良恶性肿瘤区分、转移机制）及手术、化疗、放疗等主流治疗模式与照护要领。
  • • Describe the common disorders of the integumentary system and the care required.
    详述皮肤系统常见疾患（皮炎、湿疹、银屑病、硬皮病、带状疱疹、皮肤癌等）的体征与护理措施。
  • • Describe the common musculoskeletal system disorders and the care required.
    详述肌肉骨骼系统常见疾患（关节炎、骨质疏松、痛风、纤维肌痛等）的病理改变与功能支持。
  • • Explain how to care for clients in casts and with fractures.
    阐明骨折各类型（闭合、开放、青枝、螺旋等）的愈合原理及石膏固定、牵引固定期皮肤与血液循环监测。
  • • Describe the effects of amputation and prosthesis care.
    详述截肢患者身心影响、幻肢痛应对策略、残端塑形弹力包扎及假肢日常护理与清洁要点。
  • • Describe the common neurological system disorders and the care required.
    详述神经系统常见疾患（卒中、帕金森病、亨廷顿病、多发性硬化、肌萎缩侧索硬化、癫痫）与专科照护。
  • • Identify the causes and effects of brain and spinal cord injuries and the care required.
    识别获得性脑损伤（ABI）与脊髓损伤（截瘫与四肢瘫）的致病诱因、功能丧失范围及生活自理协助原则。
  • • Describe the common endocrine system disorders and the care required.
    详述内分泌系统疾病（1型与2型糖尿病、高血糖与低血糖危象、甲状腺功能亢进与减退）的临床评估与干预。
  • • Describe the common cardiovascular system disorders and the care required.
    详述心血管系统疾病（高血压、冠状动脉粥样硬化、心绞痛、心肌梗死、充血性心力衰竭、静脉炎）与照护。
  • • Describe the common respiratory system disorders and the care required.
    详述呼吸系统疾病（慢性阻塞性肺疾病COPD、慢性支气管炎、肺气肿、哮喘、肺炎、肺结核）与吸氧支持。
  • • Describe the common immune system disorders and the care required.
    详述免疫系统常见疾病（HIV感染与艾滋病AIDS进展期、系统性红斑狼疮等自身免疫病）的防护与同理关怀。
  • • Describe the common digestive system disorders and the care required.
    详述消化系统常见疾患（胃食管反流GERD、呕吐误吸防治、胆囊炎、乳糜泻、肠易激综合征、克罗恩病、憩室病）。
  • • Describe the common urinary system disorders and the care required.
    详述泌尿系统常见疾病（尿路感染UTI、肾盂肾炎、肾结石、急慢性肾功能衰竭、透析护理要点）。
  • • Describe the common reproductive system disorders and the care required.
    详述生殖系统常见疾患（盆腔器官脱垂POP、膀胱膨出、直肠膨出、前列腺增生BPH、不孕症）的身心整体支持。
PAGE 753 · 第 753 页 Key Terms Part 1: ABI to Diverticulosis / 核心学术词汇表（第一部分：获得性脑损伤至结肠憩室病）

KEY TERMS · PART 1

核心专业学术词汇 · 第 1 部分
Chapter 33 establishes the clinical foundation for recognizing, managing, and providing empathetic support to clients living with acute, chronic, and progressive medical conditions across all organ systems. Key terms on this page cover foundational pathological concepts from acquired brain injury (ABI) through diverticulosis.
第33章为支持工作者识别、应对并同理支持罹患全身各系统急慢性及渐进性疾病的服务对象奠定了坚实的临床执业基石。本页核心专业术语涵盖从获得性脑损伤（ABI）至结肠憩室病（Diverticulosis）的基础病理学概念。
KEY TERMS · 核心专业术语表

Chapter 33 常见疾病与机体功能障碍核心病理学与临床照护词汇表

Sorrentino 原版教材第 33 章共收录 89 个涵盖传染病、肿瘤恶性转移、皮肤骨骼肌肉、神经内分泌、心脑血管循环、呼吸免疫、消化泌尿及生殖系统全人多系统核心学术解剖病理词汇。支持实时模糊检索及「紧凑表格 / 记忆卡片」双模态自由切换。

英文术语 / 病理概念 中文对照 / 原书页码 教材标准学术释义与临床界定
acquired brain injury (ABI) [acquired brain injury (abi)] 获得性脑损伤（ABI） p. 784
Damage to brain tissue caused by disease, medical condition, accident, or violence. Also known as traumatic brain injury.
获得性脑损伤（ABI）：由疾病、内科病症、事故或暴力导致的脑组织损伤。亦称创伤性颅脑损伤。
acquired immune deficiency syndrome (AIDS) [acquired immune deficiency syndrome (aids)] 获得性免疫缺陷综合征（艾滋病/AIDS） p. 800
A human immunodeficiency virus (HIV) infection that has progressed, usually because it was not treated.
获得性免疫缺陷综合征（艾滋病/AIDS）：人类免疫缺陷病毒（HIV）感染进展恶化后的终末期状态，通常因未接受有效抗病毒治疗所致。
amputation [amputation] 截肢术 p. 777
The removal of all or part of an extremity.
截肢术：切除全部或部分肢体的手术。
amyotrophic lateral sclerosis (ALS) [amyotrophic lateral sclerosis (als)] 肌萎缩侧索硬化症（渐冻症/ALS） p. 787
A degenerative disease characterized by loss of the motor neurons, with progressive weakness and atrophy of the muscles of the hands, forearms, and legs, spreading to involve most of the body and face. Also known as Lou Gehrig’s disease.
肌萎缩侧索硬化症（渐冻症/ALS）：一种以运动神经元丧失为特征的神经退行性疾病，表现为手部、前臂及下肢肌肉渐进性无力萎缩，并波及面部与全身大部分肌群。亦称卢伽雷氏病。
aneurysm [aneurysm] 动脉瘤 p. 784
A localized dilation of the wall of an artery or heart chamber.
动脉瘤：动脉管壁或心室壁的局限性病理性囊状膨出扩张。
angina pectoris [angina pectoris] 心绞痛 p. 793
Chest (pectoris) pain (angina) caused by ischemia, a lack of blood supply to the heart muscle, which is usually caused by an obstruction or spasm of the coronary arteries because of coronary artery disease.
心绞痛：因心肌缺血（供血不足）引起的胸部压榨性疼痛，通常由于冠状动脉疾病引发的血管阻塞或痉挛所致。
arrhythmia [arrhythmia] 心律失常 p. 796
Abnormal (a) heart rhythm (rhythmia).
心律失常：异常的心脏跳动节律（无规律跳动）。
arthritis [arthritis] 关节炎 p. 779
Joint (arthr) inflammation (itis).
关节炎：关节部位发生的炎症性病变。
arthroplasty [arthroplasty] 关节成形术（人工关节置换术） p. 779
Surgical replacement (plasty) of a joint (arthro).
关节成形术（人工关节置换术）：通过外科手术置换病变关节的手术。
asthma [asthma] 支气管哮喘 p. 797
A respiratory disorder characterized by recurring episodes of paroxysmal dyspnea (intermittent spasms of shortness of breath); wheezing on expiration, inspiration, or both, caused by constriction (narrowing) of bronchi; coughing; and thick bronchial secretions of mucus.
支气管哮喘：一种以反复发作的阵发性呼吸困难（突发性气短痉挛）、支气管收缩狭窄引发的呼气/吸气相哮鸣音、咳嗽及大量黏稠支气管黏液分泌为特征的呼吸系统疾病。
athlete's foot [athlete's foot] 足癣（脚气） p. 769
A highly contagious fungal skin infection that causes the affected foot to peel, burn, itch, and turn red.
足癣（脚气）：一种高传染性的足部真菌感染，引起受累脚部脱皮、灼痛、瘙痒及皮肤发红。
benign [benign] 良性 p. 766
Noncancerous.
良性：非癌性的（无恶性扩散倾向）。
benign prostatic hypertrophy (BPH) [benign prostatic hypertrophy (bph)] 良性前列腺增生（BPH） p. 807
An enlarged prostate gland.
良性前列腺增生（BPH）：男性前列腺腺体的非癌性病理性体积增大。
boil [boil] 疖肿 p. 769
See furuncle.
疖肿：见“furuncle（疖）”。
cancer [cancer] 癌症（恶性肿瘤） p. 766
A group of diseases characterized by out-of￾control cell division and growth, which can occur in many body systems.
癌症（恶性肿瘤）：一组以细胞异常失控增殖分裂为特征的疾病群，可发生于人体多个器官系统。
carrier [carrier] carrier p. 757
A person who carries a pathogen within their body that can be transmitted to others yet who remains healthy because of a strong immune response.
carrier：A person who carries a pathogen within their body that can be transmitted to others yet who remains healthy because of a strong immune response.
celiac disease [celiac disease] 乳糜泻 p. 803
An inborn metabolic disorder that results in the inability to tolerate gluten protein, which is found in wheat, oats, and barley.
乳糜泻：一种自身免疫性消化吸收障碍，患者摄入谷蛋白（麸质）后破坏小肠黏膜内壁绒毛，导致营养素严重吸收不良。
chickenpox [chickenpox] chickenpox p. 759
An illness caused by the varicella￾zoster virus that primarily occurs during childhood; diagnosed by the presence of fever and painful vesicles (fluid-filled sacs) throughout the body.
chickenpox：An illness caused by the varicella￾zoster virus that primarily occurs during childhood; diagnosed by the presence of fever and painful vesicles (fluid-filled sacs) throughout the body.
cholecystitis [cholecystitis] 胆囊炎 p. 803
Inflammation (itis) of the gallbladder (cholecyst).
胆囊炎：胆囊壁发生的急慢性炎症，常由胆囊结石嵌顿引起。
chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD) [chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (copd)] 慢性阻塞性肺疾病（慢阻肺/COPD） p. 797
A persistent lung disorder that obstructs (blocks) the airways; refers to chronic bronchitis and emphysema.
慢性阻塞性肺疾病（慢阻肺/COPD）：一种以持续性气流受限为特征的渐进性肺部疾患，阻塞呼吸道并导致呼吸困难。
cirrhosis [cirrhosis] cirrhosis p. 803
Persistent liver disease characterized by normal liver cells being replaced by scar tissue.
cirrhosis：Persistent liver disease characterized by normal liver cells being replaced by scar tissue.
colitis [colitis] 结肠炎 p. 804
Inflammation (itis) of the colon (col).
结肠炎：大肠（结肠）黏膜发生的炎症。
communicable disease [communicable disease] 传染病 p. 755
A disease caused by transmitting disease-causing pathogens from one person to another by direct contact, indirect contact, airborne transmission, vehicle transmission, or vector transmission and that usually spreads easily. Also known as contagious disease, communicable illness, infectious illness, or infectious disease.
传染病：由致病病原微生物直接或间接从一人传播至另一人的疾病。
communicable phase [communicable phase] communicable phase p. 757
The period when a person is infectious and can spread pathogens to others.
communicable phase：The period when a person is infectious and can spread pathogens to others.
congestive heart failure (CHF) [congestive heart failure (chf)] 充血性心力衰竭（心衰/CHF） p. 795
An abnormal condition that occurs when the heart cannot pump blood normally. Blood backs up and causes an abnormal amount (congestion) of fluid in the tissues. Also known as heart failure.
充血性心力衰竭（心衰/CHF）：心脏泵血功能异常受损，无法泵出满足机体代谢所需足量血液的进行性病理状态。
contagious disease [contagious disease] contagious disease p. 755
See communicable disease.
contagious disease：See communicable disease.
coronary artery disease (CAD) [coronary artery disease (cad)] 冠状动脉疾病（冠心病/CAD） p. 793
A condition of impaired cardiac pumping due to narrowed or blocked arteries.
冠状动脉疾病（冠心病/CAD）：向心肌供血的冠状动脉管腔发生狭窄或阻塞的动脉硬化性疾患。
Crohn's disease [crohn's disease] Crohn's disease p. 804
A persistent inflammatory bowel disease of unknown origin, usually affecting the ileum, the colon, or another part of the gastrointestinal (GI) tract.
Crohn's disease：A persistent inflammatory bowel disease of unknown origin, usually affecting the ileum, the colon, or another part of the gastrointestinal (GI) tract.
cyst [cyst] 囊肿 p. 769
An abnormal, closed sac, which may contain air, fluids, or semi-solid material.
囊肿：具有明确囊壁、内含液体、半流质或气体成分的闭合性囊状肿物。
cystitis [cystitis] 膀胱炎 p. 805
A bladder (cyst) infection (itis).
膀胱炎：膀胱黏膜发生的细菌性感染炎症。
cystocele [cystocele] 囊肿 p. 807
Prolapse of the bladder.
囊肿：具有明确囊壁、内含液体、半流质或气体成分的闭合性囊状肿物。
dehydration [dehydration] dehydration p. 791
A decrease in the amount of water in body tissues, which occurs when fluid output exceeds intake.
dehydration：A decrease in the amount of water in body tissues, which occurs when fluid output exceeds intake.
dermatitis [dermatitis] 皮炎 p. 770
Inflammation (itis) of the skin (derma) caused by direct contact with an irritating or allergy-causing substance.
皮炎：皮肤浅层发生的炎性病变，通常伴有红斑、瘙痒及水肿。
diabetes [diabetes] diabetes p. 788
A metabolic disorder characterized by hyperglycemia (high blood sugar levels) and resulting from low levels of insulin or a resistance to insulin’s effect at a cellular level.
diabetes：A metabolic disorder characterized by hyperglycemia (high blood sugar levels) and resulting from low levels of insulin or a resistance to insulin’s effect at a cellular level.
diverticulosis [diverticulosis] 憩室症 p. 804
The condition (osis) of having small pouches in the colon that bulge outward (diverticulum).
憩室症：大肠管壁存在小囊状袋（憩室）的状态，通常无明显临床症状。
eczema [eczema] 湿疹 p. 791
A persistent inflammatory condition of the skin that can include recurring skin rashes.
湿疹：一种以反复发作红斑皮疹、剧烈瘙痒及渗出脱屑为特征的慢性复发性炎性皮肤病。
epilepsy [epilepsy] 癫痫 p. 781
A group of neurological disorders characterized by recurrent episodes of convulsive seizures, sensory disturbances, abnormal behaviour, loss of consciousness, or some or all of these features.
癫痫：一组以脑神经元异常过度放电所致的反复阵发性抽搐发作、意识丧失或感觉异常为特征的神经系统疾病。
febrile respiratory illness (FRI) [febrile respiratory illness (fri)] 发热性呼吸道疾病（FRI） p. 759
A term used to describe a wide range of respiratory infections, such as colds, influenza, influenza-like illness (ILI), and pneumonia, which are spread through droplets.
发热性呼吸道疾病（FRI）：用于描述一系列以发热为特征并通过飞沫传播的呼吸道感染性疾病统称（如流感、普通感冒等）。
fibromyalgia [fibromyalgia] 纤维肌痛综合征 p. 781
A condition associated with aching, stiffness, and fatigue in muscles, ligaments, and tendons.
纤维肌痛综合征：一种以全身广泛性肌肉骨骼慢性弥漫性疼痛、僵硬、疲乏无力及睡眠障碍为特征的慢性综合征。
fracture [fracture] 骨折 p. 774
A broken bone.
骨折：骨骼结构的完整性与连续性发生部分或完全断裂中断。
furuncle [furuncle] 疖（疖肿） p. 769
A skin disorder caused by the infection of a hair follicle. Also called a boil.
疖（疖肿）：由毛囊及深部周围组织细菌感染化脓坏死形成的急性炎性硬结疼痛结节。
gastro-esophageal reflux disease (GERD) [gastro-esophageal reflux disease (gerd)] 胃食管反流病（GERD） p. 803
A disorder of the digestive system that causes heartburn.
胃食管反流病（GERD）：胃内容物异常逆流入食管引发烧心胸痛及食管黏膜损伤的慢性反流病症。
gout [gout] 痛风 p. 780
A disease caused by the accumulation of uric acid in the cartilage of a joint, especially in the tendons. Also called metabolic arthritis.
痛风：因体内嘌呤代谢紊乱导致血尿酸水平过高、单钠尿酸盐晶体沉积于关节软骨引起的极度剧痛性关节炎。
heart attack [heart attack] heart attack p. 794
See myocardial infarction (MI).
heart attack：See myocardial infarction (MI).
hemiplegia [hemiplegia] 偏瘫 p. 783
Paralysis (plegia) of one side (hemi) of the body; the arm and leg, body organs, vision, the tongue, and the swallowing mechanisms on the affected side can all be compromised.
偏瘫：身体一侧（左半身或右半身）肢体肌力的完全或部分丧失。
hepatitis [hepatitis] 肝炎 p. 761
Inflammation (itis) of the liver (hepat) that may be caused by bacterial or viral infection, parasitic infestation, alcohol, drugs, toxins, transfusion of incompatible (not matching) blood type, consuming contaminated water or food, using dirty needles or syringes, or practising unsafe sex.
肝炎：肝脏实质组织发生的急慢性炎症。
hives [hives] hives p. 773
See urticaria.
hives：See urticaria.
human immunodeficiency virus (HIV) [human immunodeficiency virus (hiv)] 人类免疫缺陷病毒（HIV） p. 800
A potentially fatal infection caused by a virus that attacks the body’s immune system, making it unable to fight off certain infections.
人类免疫缺陷病毒（HIV）：特异性侵袭并逐步破坏人体免疫系统CD4+T淋巴细胞的逆转录病毒，终末期可发展为艾滋病。
Huntington's disease [huntington's disease] Huntington's disease p. 786
An inherited neurological disorder that causes uncontrolled movements, emotional disturbances, and cognitive losses.
Huntington's disease：An inherited neurological disorder that causes uncontrolled movements, emotional disturbances, and cognitive losses.
hyperthyroidism [hyperthyroidism] 甲状腺功能亢进症（甲亢） p. 789
A condition caused by an overactive thyroid gland.
甲状腺功能亢进症（甲亢）：甲状腺腺体合成并分泌过多甲状腺激素引起的高代谢状态。
hypothyroidism [hypothyroidism] 甲状腺功能减退症（甲减） p. 790
A condition caused by an underactive thyroid gland.
甲状腺功能减退症（甲减）：甲状腺腺体合成与分泌甲状腺激素不足引发的代谢低下状态。
impetigo [impetigo] 脓疱疮（黄水疮） p. 772
A contagious skin disorder caused by bacteria.
脓疱疮（黄水疮）：由金黄色葡萄球菌或链球菌引起的极具接触传染性的浅表化脓性皮肤感染，好发于面部。
infertility [infertility] 不孕不育症 p. 808
A lack of conception (getting pregnant) after a reasonable period of sexual intercourse without the use of contraception or period of medical fertilization treatment (1 year for women who are under 35 years of age, 6 months for women over 35 years of age).
不孕不育症：未采取避孕措施进行规律性生活满一年仍未能受孕。
influenza (“the flu”) [influenza (“the flu”)] 流行性感冒（流感） p. 798
A highly contagious infection of the respiratory tract by the causative (disease￾causing) virus in airborne droplets. Symptoms include sore throat, cough, fever, muscular pain, and weakness.
流行性感冒（流感）：由流感病毒引起的急性高度传染性呼吸系统疾病。
irritable bowel syndrome (IBS) [irritable bowel syndrome (ibs)] 肠易激综合征（IBS） p. 803
A disorder of the bowel characterized by abdominal pain and changes in bowel habits.
肠易激综合征（IBS）：一种以反复发作腹痛、腹胀及排便习惯改变（腹泻或便秘）为特征的功能性肠道疾病。
Kegel exercises Pelvic floor muscle-training exercises performed to strengthen [kegel exercises pelvic floor muscle-training exercises performed to strengthen] Kegel exercises Pelvic floor muscle-training exercises performed to strengthen p. 808
the muscles of the pelvic floor that support the vagina, bladder, and urethra. Also known as pelvic floor exercises.
Kegel exercises Pelvic floor muscle-training exercises performed to strengthen：the muscles of the pelvic floor that support the vagina, bladder, and urethra. Also known as pelvic floor exercises.
Lou Gehrig's disease [lou gehrig's disease] Lou Gehrig's disease p. 787
See amyotrophic lateral sclerosis.
Lou Gehrig's disease：See amyotrophic lateral sclerosis.
malignant Describes any condition in [malignant describes any condition in] 恶性（癌性） p. 766
the body that, if untreated, may be a threat to health (e.g., malignant hypertension). Specifically, it describes a tumour that invades and destroys other cells or tissues. Also known as cancerous.
恶性（癌性）：具有侵袭破坏周围健康组织并向远处器官播散转移特性的癌性病变。
metastasis [metastasis] 转移 p. 766
The spread of cancer to other parts of the body.
转移：癌细胞脱离原发病灶，经由血管或淋巴系统扩散至身体其他遥远部位形成继发肿瘤的过程。
multiple sclerosis (MS) [multiple sclerosis (ms)] 多发性硬化（MS） p. 786
A persistent, slowly progressive disease in which the body’s immune system attacks the protective myelin sheaths that surround the nerve cells of the brain and spinal cord (a process called demyelination), resulting in damaged areas that are unable to transmit nerve impulses.
多发性硬化（MS）：一种自身免疫性中枢神经系统脱髓鞘疾病，脑和脊髓神经纤维髓鞘受损破坏神经信号传导。
myocardial infarction (MI) [myocardial infarction (mi)] 心肌梗死（心梗/MI） p. 794
Death (infarction) of heart tissue (myocardium) caused by lack of oxygen to the heart. Also known as heart attack.
心肌梗死（心梗/MI）：因冠状动脉急性血流中断导致部分心肌组织严重缺氧发生缺血性坏死。俗称心脏病发作。
osteoarthritis (OA) [osteoarthritis (oa)] 骨关节炎（退行性关节病/OA） p. 779
A type of arthritis that causes the cartilage that occupies the joints of the body to wear away, causing pain in and disfigurement of the joints.
骨关节炎（退行性关节病/OA）：关节软骨发生渐进性磨损退化变薄，导致骨端摩擦、剧烈关节疼痛及骨赘畸形的退行性病变。
osteomyelitis [osteomyelitis] 骨髓炎 p. 776
Inflammation (itis) or infection of the bone (osteo).
骨髓炎：致病细菌侵入骨骼及骨髓腔内部引起的严重感染性炎症。
osteoporosis [osteoporosis] 骨质疏松症 p. 779
A bone disorder (osteo) in which the bone becomes porous and brittle (poros).
骨质疏松症：骨组织量减少、骨微结构破坏导致骨骼变薄变脆极易发生脆性骨折的全身骨骼代谢疾患。
paralysis [paralysis] 瘫痪 p. 787
Complete or partial loss of ability to move a limb or muscle group.
瘫痪：肢体或特定肌群随意运动功能的完全或部分丧失。
paraplegia [paraplegia] 截瘫 p. 787
Paralysis (plegia) from the waist down.
截瘫：因胸腰段脊髓损伤导致的下半身双下肢运动与感觉功能丧失。
Parkinson's disease [parkinson's disease] Parkinson's disease p. 785
Neurological disorder in which the cells in certain parts of the brain are gradually destroyed; causes tremors, muscle stiffness, slow movement, and poor balance. In advanced cases, it can also cause dementia.
Parkinson's disease：Neurological disorder in which the cells in certain parts of the brain are gradually destroyed; causes tremors, muscle stiffness, slow movement, and poor balance. In advanced cases, it can also cause dementia.
pathogen [pathogen] 病原体 p. 755
A microbe that can cause illness, infection, or disease.
病原体：能引起人体感染、生病或组织器质性病变的致病微生物。
pelvic organ [pelvic organ] 盆腔器官脱垂（POP） p. 807
prolapse (POP) When one or more pelvic organs slip down from their normal position and into the vagina because their supportive tissues have become weak.
盆腔器官脱垂（POP）：盆底肌肉韧带筋膜萎缩松弛薄弱，导致膀胱、子宫或直肠等一个或多个器官脱出移位滑入阴道。
phlebitis [phlebitis] 静脉炎 p. 796
An inflammation (itis) of a vein (phleb).
静脉炎：静脉血管壁发生的无菌性或感染性炎症反应。
pneumonia [pneumonia] 肺炎 p. 797
Infection of the lung tissue.
肺炎：细菌、病毒等病原体或误吸引发的肺泡与肺间质急性渗出性感染。
progressive illness [progressive illness] 进行性疾病 p. 755
An illness that gets worse over time.
进行性疾病：病情随着时间推移逐渐恶化、功能不可逆衰退的疾病。
psoriasis [psoriasis] 银屑病（牛皮癣） p. 772
A persistent skin disorder that affects the skin and joints. It commonly causes red scaly patches to appear on the skin.
银屑病（牛皮癣）：一种慢性复发性自身免疫性皮肤病，以皮肤表面反复出现界限清晰红斑覆有厚层银白色鳞屑为特征。
pyelonephritis [pyelonephritis] 肾盂肾炎 p. 805
Inflammation (itis) of the kidney (nephr) and renal pelvis (pyelo) because of bacterial infection.
肾盂肾炎：细菌逆行上行侵入肾盂及肾间质实质引起的急性或慢性严重泌尿道感染。
quadriplegia [quadriplegia] 四肢瘫 p. 787
Paralysis (plegia) of all four (quad) limbs and the trunk; depending on the location and severity of the spinal cord injury, the client can be paralyzed from the neck down.
四肢瘫：颈段脊髓受损导致躯干及四肢全部随意运动与感觉功能完全或部分丧失。
rectocele [rectocele] 直肠膨出 p. 807
Prolapse of the rectum.
直肠膨出：直肠前壁向阴道后壁突出膨出的盆底松弛脱垂病症。
renal calculi [renal calculi] 肾结石 p. 805
Kidney (renal) stones (calculi).
肾结石：尿液矿物质晶体盐类析出沉积形成的坚硬结石。
scabies [scabies] 疥疮 p. 772
A highly contagious skin infection caused by a mite.
疥疮：由疥螨在表皮角质层内穿掘隧道引起的高度传染性寄生虫皮肤病，夜间剧烈奇痒。
scleroderma [scleroderma] 硬皮病 p. 772
A progressive autoimmune disease that involves the hardening (sclero) of the skin (derma) and connective tissue throughout the body.
硬皮病：一种以皮肤及全身内脏结缔组织纤维化硬化胶原过度沉积为特征的慢性进展性自身免疫病。
seborrheic keratosis [seborrheic keratosis] 脂溢性角化病（老年疣） p. 773
Noncancerous skin growth that is common in older persons.
脂溢性角化病（老年疣）：老年人最常见的良性表皮增生性皮肤肿瘤，表现为褐色疣状扁平斑块。

infections (STIs) Diseases that are spread through sexual contact.
性传播感染（STIs）：主要通过性接触途径在人与人之间传播的一组感染性疾病。
shingles [shingles] 带状疱疹 p. 773
A viral infection causing a painful rash of raised blisters, which can appear anywhere on the body, usually on one side. Any person who has had chickenpox is at risk of developing shingles. It is caused by herpes varicella-zoster, the same virus that causes chicken pox.
带状疱疹：水痘-带状疱疹病毒在神经节潜伏多年后重新激活引起的沿特定单侧周围神经分布的簇集水疱与剧烈神经痛。

A temporary interruption of blood flow to the brain.
短暂性脑缺血发作（小中风/TIA）：脑动脉局部供血发生短暂、过性受阻引起突发局灶性神经功能缺损，通常在24小时内完全恢复。
traumatic brain injury [traumatic brain injury] 创伤性脑损伤 p. 784
See acquired brain injury (ABI).
创伤性脑损伤：见“acquired brain injury (ABI)（获得性脑损伤）”。
tuberculosis (TB) [tuberculosis (tb)] 结核病（肺结核/TB） p. 799
A persistent bacterial infection generally transmitted by the inhalation or ingestion of infected droplets and usually affecting the lungs, although infection of multiple organ systems occurs.
结核病（肺结核/TB）：由结核分枝杆菌引起的慢性传染病，主要通过呼吸道飞沫传播侵犯肺部，亦可波及全身多器官。
tumour [tumour] 肿瘤 p. 766
A new growth of abnormal cells that could be malignant or benign.
肿瘤：机体异常细胞失控异常增殖形成的占位性新生物，可分为良性与恶性。
urinary tract [urinary tract] 尿路感染（UTI） p. 805
infection (UTI) An infection in the urinary system.
尿路感染（UTI）：病原微生物在肾脏、输尿管、膀胱或尿道黏膜繁殖引起的感染性炎症。
urticaria [urticaria] 荨麻疹（风疹块） p. 773
A common skin condition characterized by raised, usually itchy wheals. It is commonly caused by an allergic reaction to food, food additives, or drugs. Also known as hives.
荨麻疹（风疹块）：因皮肤黏膜小血管扩张渗透性增加引发的突发性、隆起瘙痒性局限性水肿风团。

A circular, red, spongy lesion that evolves (develops) and changes over minutes to hours. It is usually surrounded by an area of redness called a flare.
风团：圆形或椭圆形隆起发红的海绵状水肿性皮肤损害，可在数分钟至数小时内迅速发生、消退，常伴有充血红晕。

INTRODUCTION: UNDERSTANDING ILLNESS AND SUPPORT WORK PRIORITIES

引言：理解疾病本质与支持工作核心优先事项
In Chapter 32 you were introduced to the human body’s many systems. Building upon that information, this chapter presents subject areas such as communicable diseases, cancer, and common illnesses that affect each body system. A review of Chapters 19 and 32 will be helpful when studying this chapter. Refer to Chapters 34, 35, 37, and 38 for descriptions of health conditions not discussed in this chapter.
在第32章中，您已系统学习了人体的各大解剖与生理系统。在此基础上，本章将深入阐述传染病、癌症以及侵犯机体各大系统的常见临床疾患。研读本章时，温习第19章（感染控制）与第32章将大有裨益。关于视听障碍、认知功能损害、心理健康及急重症，请参阅第34、35、37和38章。
Some illnesses that are discussed are acute, others are chronic or persistent, and some others are progressive illnesses—that is, they gradually get worse over time. Others can be disabling or life-threatening. Some clients with progressive, disabling, or life-threatening conditions become immobile and bedridden. When caring for clients with any illness, it is important that you consistently provide empathetic, client-centred care; apply the priorities of support work (see the Providing Compassionate Care: Caring for Clients With Illnesses or Health Conditions box); and communicate effectively to the client, their family, and the rest of the health care team (see Chapters 10, 11, and 26).
本章探讨的疾患中，部分为发病急骤的急性病（Acute），部分为长期迁延的慢性或持续性疾病（Chronic/Persistent），还有一部分属于进行性加重的渐进性疾病（Progressive illnesses——随时间推移逐渐恶化）。许多病症具有致残性甚至危及生命。部分处于进行性、致残性或终末期的服务对象可能会丧失活动能力而长期卧床。照护任何疾病患者时，您必须始终贯彻充满同理心、以服务对象为中心的照护模式；恪守支持工作核心优先事项（见《提供富有同理心的照护：照护罹患疾病或健康问题的服务对象》专栏）；并与服务对象、家属及跨学科医疗团队保持高效顺畅的沟通（详见第10、11和26章）。

COMMUNICABLE DISEASES: GENERAL PRINCIPLES

传染病：基本原理与致病微生物传播
A pathogen is a microbe that can cause illness, infection, or disease (see Chapter 19). A communicable disease or illness is caused when disease-causing pathogens are transmitted from one person to another by direct contact, indirect contact, airborne transmission, vehicle transmission, or vector transmission (see TABLE 19.1: Modes of Transmission of Microorganisms on page 788) and usually spread easily. They are also known as communicable illnesses, infectious illnesses, or infectious diseases.
病原体（Pathogen）是指能侵入机体并引起疾病、感染或功能失调的微生物（见第19章）。当致病病原体通过直接接触、间接接触、空气飞沫传播、媒介物传播或生物病媒传播，从一个人传递给另一个人并极易发生扩散时，便构成了传染病（Communicable disease）。在临床上，传染病亦常被称为接触传染病（Contagious disease）、感染性疾患（Infectious illnesses）或传染性疾病。
PAGE 756 · 第 756 页 Chain of Infection & Compassionate Care in Illness / 打破感染链与疾病照护中的同理心准则
Communicable diseases included in this textbook include COVID-19 (see Chapter 19), H1N1, hepatitis, sexually transmitted infections (STIs) (see pages 764–766); influenza and tuberculosis (TB), which are discussed under Respiratory System Disorders (page 797); and human immunodeficiency virus (HIV and AIDS), which is discussed under Immune System Disorders (page 800). A review of Chapter 19, including topics such as Standard Practices, and how support workers can break the chain of infection is recommended.
本教材系统涵盖的传染病包括：新冠病毒（COVID-19，见第19章）、H1N1甲型流感、病毒性肝炎、性传播感染（STIs，见第764–766页）；在呼吸系统疾病章节中探讨的流行性感冒与肺结核（TB，见第797页）；以及在免疫系统疾病章节中详述的人类免疫缺陷病毒与艾滋病（HIV/AIDS，见第800页）。建议学习者复习第19章中关于常规防护操作（Standard Practices）以及支持工作者如何阻断感染链（Breaking the chain of infection）的核心规程。

Three Possible Outcomes of Exposure to a Pathogen

暴露于病原体后的三种可能临床结局
Being exposed to pathogens does not always result in an infection, because the body can protect itself from infection. There are three possible outcomes after exposure to a pathogen: 1. The immune system destroys the pathogen. The pathogen does not live and grow within the body, so an infection does not occur (see Chapter 32 to review discussion about the immune system). If the person has been infected by that pathogen previously, their body may have 'immunological memory,' thereby fighting the infection without causing symptoms. 2. The immune system does not destroy the pathogen, and yet an infection does not develop. Some people remain healthy because of a strong immune response. They are healthy and have no other illnesses or infections to deal with, but the pathogen can still be present within the body and can be transmitted to others; these persons would be considered carriers. The pathogens are not harmful to these persons but might be harmful to others who have a weakened immune system for certain reasons, such as fatigue, stress, malnourishment, or other illnesses. 3. An infection develops sometime after exposure to the pathogen (see next page).
暴露于病原体并不意味着必然会发生感染，因为机体自身拥有抵御感染的多层保护防线。暴露于致病微生物后，通常会出现以下三种转归结局： 1. 免疫系统彻底消灭病原体：病原体无法在体内定植繁衍，因而不会发生临床感染（参阅第32章关于免疫系统的论述）。若机体既往曾接触过该病原体，免疫细胞具备「免疫记忆」，能迅速将其清除而不表现出任何临床症状。 2. 免疫系统未能彻底杀灭病原体，但机体亦未发病（健康携带状态）：部分人群由于自身免疫抗病力较强，病原体在体内潜伏共存却不引发组织损伤。然而，病原体仍可随分泌物排出体外并传染他人，此类个体在医学上被称为「病原体携带者」（Carriers）。病原体对其自身无害，但一旦传递给因过度劳累、压力、营养不良或基础疾病导致免疫力低下的易感人群，则会诱发严重感染。 3. 经过一定潜伏期后机体发病（见下一页）。

Providing emotional support and active listening promotes the client's dignity and holistic well-being.

倾听服务对象倾诉并给予真诚的情感支持，能够极大维护其做人的尊严并增进整体身心安适。

PAGE 757 · 第 757 页 Incubation Period, Communicable Phase & Immunization / 潜伏期、传染期界定与疫苗预防学机制

INCUBATION PERIOD AND COMMUNICABLE PHASE

潜伏期与传染期：病理时相与传播风险界定
Outcome 3 (continued): An infection develops sometime after exposure to the pathogen. The period between exposure to the pathogen and the onset (beginning) of illness is called the incubation period, which may be relatively short (hours to days) or may last for years. During the incubation period, the person can transfer the pathogen to others. The time during which a person is at risk of transferring pathogens to others (putting people at risk of 'catching' the illness) is known as the communicable phase. The incubation period and length of time of the communicable phase vary from pathogen to pathogen.
结局 3（续）：暴露于病原体后经历一定时间最终引发临床感染。从初次接触病原体到临床症状最初出现之间的这段时间窗口，被称为「潜伏期」（Incubation period）。潜伏期长短因病原体而异，有的极其短暂（数小时至数天，如急性胃肠炎），有的可长达数年（如结核菌或HIV）。至关重要的是：在潜伏期内，感染者体内病原体大量复制并向外排毒，已具备传染他人的能力！感染者具备向外界播散病原体、使他人面临被传染风险的时间段，被称为「传染期」（Communicable phase）。不同病原体的潜伏期长短与传染期跨度具有显著差异。

Vaccinations as a Preventative Measure

疫苗接种作为现代医学一级预防手段
Vaccinations have been proven to prevent communicable (contagious) illnesses from developing into more serious illness for people who have been vaccinated against them. There are a number of common communicable childhood illnesses (such as measles, mumps, and chickenpox) that babies and toddlers can be vaccinated against. Updated immunization schedules are available from family doctors, local health units, or the Public Health Agency of Canada website (http://www.phac-aspc.gc.ca/publicat/cig-gci/p01-12-eng.php#tab1).
大量循证医学证据已充分证实，规范接种疫苗（Vaccinations）能有效诱导人体特异性体液免疫与细胞免疫，阻断传染病的发生或大幅降低重症与死亡发生率。诸多高发于儿童期的烈性传染病（如麻疹、流行性腮腺炎、水痘及脊髓灰质炎等），均已纳入婴幼儿常规免疫规划程序。加拿大各省份最新版免疫接种程序表可直接向家庭医生、当地公共卫生局或加拿大公共卫生署（PHAC）官方渠道获取。

Pandemic Prevention and Public Health Surveillance

大流行防范与公共卫生疾控监测
A pandemic occurs when a communicable disease or infection spreads quickly throughout the population of a country, across continents, or throughout the world, affecting very large numbers of people. Epidemics are localized spikes in disease incidence, whereas pandemics cross international borders with sustained community transmission. Public health agencies closely monitor transmission dynamics, implement case-contact tracing, enforce quarantine guidelines, and distribute vaccines during pandemic emergencies.
当某种传染性疾患在某一国家的人群中迅速蔓延、跨越各大洲界限甚至席卷全球并侵袭庞大人群时，在流行病学上被界定为「大流行」（Pandemic）。局限在特定区域的疾病突发激增被称为地方性流行（Epidemic），而大流行则打破了国家地理边界并在全球社区广泛持续扩散。在突发公共卫生事件与大流行暴发期间，公共卫生机构通过严密监控传播动力学、推行病例密切接触者追踪排查、执行检疫隔离指南并集中调度分发疫苗来控制疫情蔓延。
PAGE 758 · 第 758 页 Adult Communicable Diseases & Occupational Health Protocols / 成人高发传染病与从业人员职业防护规程

COMMUNICABLE DISEASES IN ADULTS & WORKPLACE SCREENING

成人高发传染病与支持工作者职业筛查要求
While childhood diseases are well known, there are specific communicable diseases that pose grave occupational and clinical risks in adult populations, especially within long-term care facilities, retirement homes, and community healthcare settings. Two crucial institutional health protocols for healthcare workers are: • Hepatitis B immunization: Healthcare employers strongly recommend or require staff to receive the full Hepatitis B vaccine series, especially if workers handle linen, body fluids, or food products. Check with your local health unit or employer occupational health department for vaccine access. • Two-step Tuberculin Skin Testing (TST): Tuberculosis (TB) is a highly infectious, airborne bacterial disease caused by Mycobacterium tuberculosis. Support workers must immediately notify their physician and supervisor if they suspect exposure. Because many older residents in long-term care may have had latent TB exposure in their youth, healthcare institutions mandate that all new staff and incoming residents complete a two-step Mantoux tuberculin skin test (or chest radiography, if contraindicated) upon hire/admission to rule out active pulmonary disease.
尽管儿童期传染病广为人知，但在成年人群——特别是在长期护理机构（LTC）、养老院和社区照护环境中，特定传染病带来了极为严峻的职业暴露与交叉感染风险。卫生系统对医护与支持工作人员设立了两项核心前置筛查规程： • 乙型肝炎主动免疫接种：医疗与护理雇主强烈建议甚至强制要求所有工作人员完成乙肝疫苗全套接种，特别是对于接触衣物被褥、体液或参与食物准备的人员。务必向当地公共卫生局或雇主职业健康部门咨询接种方案。 • 结核菌素两步皮肤试验（Two-step TST）：肺结核是由结核分枝杆菌引起的烈性空气飞沫传播性传染病。作为支持工作者，若怀疑自身暴露于结核疑似患者或接触者，必须立即向医生及主管报告。鉴于许多长期照护机构的高龄长者年轻时可能接触过结核病菌（潜伏感染），加拿大法规强制规定所有新入职员工及新入住院长者必须完成结核菌素「两步法皮肤试验」（Mantoux 试验，若有禁忌症则必须拍摄胸部X光平片），彻底排除活动性肺结核，切断机构内暴发隐患。

OVERVIEW OF COVID-19 IN HEALTHCARE SETTINGS

新型冠状病毒肺炎（COVID-19）院感防控要领
COVID-19 is a highly contagious, airborne, communicable viral disease that is spread mainly through close contact from person to person, including between people who are physically near each other (within about 2 metres [6 feet]). Symptoms may take up to 14 days after exposure to a person infected with COVID-19 to appear, though the average incubation period is approximately 3 to 7 days.
新型冠状病毒肺炎（COVID-19）是一种由冠状病毒引起的高传染性、通过空气微粒及气溶胶飞沫传播的传染病。其主要经由人与人之间的近距离接触传播，特别是在相距2米（约6英尺）范围内的密切互动。从暴露接触感染者到出现临床症状的潜伏期最长可达14天，平均潜伏期通常为3至7天。
PAGE 759 · 第 759 页 Table 33.1: Communicable Diseases in Canada (Part 1) / 表 33.1 加拿大常见传染病特征、传播途径与照护一览（第1部分）

TABLE 33.1: COMMON COMMUNICABLE DISEASES IN CANADA (PART 1)

表 33.1：加拿大常见传染病综合鉴别与照护要览（第 1 部分）
PAGE 760 · 第 760 页 COVID-19 Epidemiology, Transmission & Variants / 新冠病毒流行病学、传播动力学与变异株防控

COVID-19 TRANSMISSION DYNAMICS AND SPREAD

新冠病毒微粒气溶胶传播机制与人畜共患风险
COVID-19 is spread when an infected person breathes, talks, coughs, or sneezes, producing respiratory droplets and aerosols that can be inhaled by people nearby or land in their eyes, nose, or mouth. In indoor and poorly ventilated settings, microscopic aerosols can linger in the air and travel distances greater than 2 metres. Transmission can also occur if a person touches a contaminated surface (fomite) and then touches their own face without performing hand hygiene, though airborne inhalation remains the primary driver of transmission.
新冠病毒主要在感染者呼吸、说话、咳嗽或打喷嚏时，释放出大量含有病毒的呼吸道飞沫与微小气溶胶（Aerosols）。这些微粒可被邻近人员吸入肺部，或直接溅入其眼结膜、鼻黏膜或口腔。在密闭及通风不良的室内环境中，微米级气溶胶微粒能悬浮空中长时间漂移，传播距离远超2米。尽管触摸污染物体表面后再触碰面部亦可发生间接接触传播，但空气呼吸道吸入始终是主导性的绝对传播链条。
Rare zoonotic transmissions have also been documented: a small number of domestic pets worldwide, including cats and dogs, have been reported to be infected with COVID-19, mostly after sustained close contact with people who had COVID-19. Healthcare workers caring for COVID-19 clients in private homes must ensure that pets are separated from the care environment to prevent cross-contamination.
此外，全球医学界亦监测到极罕见的人畜共患传播案例：全球已有极少数家养宠物（包括猫和犬类）被确诊感染新冠病毒，其绝大多数源于与确诊家庭成员长期近距离密切接触。在居家社区为新冠患者提供照护的支持工作者，必须建议家属将宠物暂时隔离在照护区域之外，避免皮毛携带或交叉污染。
Although its first cases were identified in late 2019, COVID-19 was officially designated a pandemic virus in early 2020. Since then, the SARS-CoV-2 coronavirus has undergone continuous genetic mutation, generating successive Variants of Concern (VOCs, such as Alpha, Delta, and Omicron lineages). Variants exhibit enhanced transmissibility, shorter incubation periods, and partial immune evasion against earlier antibodies, requiring ongoing vaccine updates (booster doses) and vigilant infection control practices.
尽管首批病例于2019年底被报告，但COVID-19在2020年初被世界卫生组织正式定性为全球大流行病毒。此后，SARS-CoV-2新型冠状病毒在人群传播中不断发生基因突变，相继演变出多种「受关注变异株」（Variants of Concern，如Alpha、Delta及奥密克戎Omicron系列变异株）。这些变异株通常呈现更强的传染性、更短的潜伏代际以及对既往中和抗体的部分免疫逃逸，因而需要定期更新加强针疫苗，并要求医疗机构持续保持最高级别的感控戒备。
PAGE 761 · 第 761 页 COVID-19 Care, Microvascular Clotting & Long-COVID / 新冠临床照护、微血管血栓并发症与长新冠综合征

COVID-19 PATHOPHYSIOLOGY: MULTI-ORGAN IMPACT AND BLOOD CLOTTING

新冠病理生理：多器官损伤、内皮炎与高凝血栓形成
While initially perceived as a purely respiratory illness, COVID-19 is fundamentally a systemic vascular and inflammatory disease that damages the vascular endothelium (endothelial lining of blood vessels): • Hypercoagulability & Micro-clots: COVID-19 triggers severe systemic inflammation that causes platelets and blood cells to clump together, forming abnormal clots. While massive macro-thrombi cause catastrophic pulmonary embolisms, acute myocardial infarctions, and ischemic strokes, much of the insidious heart and lung damage is caused by microscopic micro-clots that lodge in the capillaries of the myocardium and alveolar septa, cutting off tissue oxygenation. • Multi-Organ Sequelae: Beyond the respiratory tract, blood clots and vascular injury can severely compromise the kidneys (acute kidney injury), liver, lower extremities (deep vein thrombosis / DVT), and central nervous system (encephalopathy, confusion, stroke).
尽管新冠病毒感染最初被视为单纯的呼吸道疾病，但现代医学病理证实其本质是一种引起全身血管内皮损伤与严重细胞因子级联反应的系统性血管炎疾病： • 血液高凝状态与微血栓形成：新冠病毒引发全身性炎症风暴，促使血小板异常活化、纤维蛋白原大量沉积，导致血液极度高凝。大血管血栓可直接引发致命性肺栓塞、急性心肌梗死及大面积缺血性脑卒中；而更为隐匿的心肺器质性损害，则源自无数微米级「微血栓」堵塞了心肌间质与肺泡毛细血管网，导致组织弥漫性缺血坏死。 • 多脏器受累并发症：除肺部外，血栓微循环障碍还会重创肾脏（急性肾损伤甚至需透析）、肝脏转氨酶骤升、双下肢深静脉血栓（DVT）以及中枢神经系统（意识模糊、谵妄、脑膜脑炎及卒中）。

Long-COVID (Post-COVID Condition) and PSW Support

长新冠（新冠后综合征）特征与支持工作者照护策略
Post-COVID-19 condition, commonly referred to as 'Long-COVID,' affects an estimated 10% to 20% of infected individuals. Symptoms persist or recur for weeks, months, or years after the initial infection, regardless of whether the acute illness was mild or severe. Common manifestations include profound chronic fatigue not relieved by rest, post-exertional malaise (PEM), shortness of breath on exertion, cognitive dysfunction ('brain fog'), chronic cough, joint pain, chest pain, and sleep disturbances. Support Worker Care Considerations for Long-COVID: 1. Energy Conservation & Pacing: Assist the client to pace daily activities. Break ADLs into manageable increments with scheduled rest periods between bathing, dressing, and eating. 2. Fall Prevention: Cognitive fatigue and orthostatic intolerance increase fall risk. Ensure grab bars, non-slip footwear, and clutter-free pathways are maintained. 3. Validation & Empathy: Long-COVID can be frustrating and isolating. Validate the client's lived experience, avoid minimizing their exhaustion, and report changes in functional tolerance to the supervisor.
新冠后状态（Post-COVID Condition），通称「长新冠」（Long-COVID），累及约10%至20%的感染者。无论急性期感染属于轻症还是重症，各种致残性症状均可在数周、数月甚至数年后持续存在或反复波动。其典型临床表现包括：休息无法缓解的深度慢性疲劳、劳力后极度不适（PEM）、轻微活动后气促喘憋、认知记忆迟钝障碍（「脑雾」Brain fog）、顽固慢性咳嗽、游走性关节痛、心前区隐痛及重度睡眠障碍。 支持工作者针对长新冠患者的照护原则： 1. 能量守恒与节奏调节（Energy Conservation & Pacing）：协助患者合理分配体能精力。将穿衣、沐浴、进食等日常生活活动分解为碎片化轻度步骤，在每个环节之间严格插入平卧休息间隙。 2. 严防跌倒坠床：脑雾注意力涣散及体位性心动过速/低血压极易引发眩晕跌倒。确保床档稳固、卫生间安装安全扶手、穿着防滑袜并保持行走通道绝对畅通。 3. 情感共情与心理赋能：长新冠的慢性折磨极易导致患者抑郁与绝望。真诚倾听并接纳其痛苦诉求，切勿将其疲惫误判为「装病或消极」，密切观察其耐受力改变并及时向主管护士动态汇报。
PAGE 762 · 第 762 页 Hepatitis A, B, C, D & E Pathology & Transmission Routes / 病毒性肝炎各型病理、传播途径与黄疸体征

VIRAL HEPATITIS: PATHOLOGY, TYPES, AND TRANSMISSION

病毒性肝炎：病理学分型、传播途径与典型体征
Hepatitis literally means inflammation (itis) of the liver (hepat). It can be caused by bacterial or viral infections, parasitic infestation, alcohol abuse, certain prescription and illicit drugs, industrial toxins, or autoimmune attacks. The most common infectious causes are the hepatitis viruses: Hepatitis A (HAV), Hepatitis B (HBV), Hepatitis C (HCV), Hepatitis D (HDV), and Hepatitis E (HEV). Regardless of virus type, clinical signs often include fatigue, malaise, anorexia, nausea, abdominal discomfort in the right upper quadrant, fever, dark-coloured urine (bilirubinuria), light-coloured clay-like stools, and jaundice (yellowing of the sclera of the eyes and skin due to bilirubin accumulation).
肝炎（Hepatitis）字面释义为肝脏（hepat）的炎症（itis）。其致病因素包括细菌或病毒感染、寄生虫侵袭、长期酗酒、肝毒性药物或毒物、输注不相容血液以及自身免疫紊乱。临床上最普遍的传染性病因是肝炎病毒群，包括甲型（HAV）、乙型（HBV）、丙型（HCV）、丁型（HDV）及戊型（HEV）。无论何种病毒型别，肝炎的经典临床表现均包括：乏力嗜睡、食欲减退、恶心呕吐、右上腹隐痛不适、低热、浓茶样深色尿（胆红素尿）、陶土样浅色大便，以及最为显著的「黄疸」（Jaundice——体内胆红素代谢障碍在巩膜和全身皮肤沉积变黄）。

Hepatitis A (HAV): Fecal–Oral Transmission & PSW Food Safety

甲型肝炎（HAV）：粪-口传播途径与饮食排泄照护要领
Hepatitis A virus (HAV) is transmitted primarily through the fecal–oral route—that is, ingestion of food, water, or ice cubes contaminated with microscopic amounts of infected feces. Common sources include raw or undercooked shellfish harvested from sewage-polluted waters, unwashed fruits and vegetables, and food handled by infected persons who did not perform thorough hand hygiene after using the toilet. Crowded living conditions and inadequate sanitation significantly increase transmission risks. PSW Care Considerations for Hepatitis A: • Fecal Contamination Protocols: Clients with fecal incontinence, cognitive confusion, or dementia can easily contaminate bedding, clothing, bed rails, and surrounding furniture. Support workers must inspect the environment meticulously, clean and disinfect soiled areas immediately using approved germicides, wear gloves and gowns when handling stool, and perform meticulous hand hygiene. • Handwashing: Always wash hands with soap and water after handling bedpans, commodes, or contaminated linens, and especially prior to preparing food or assisting clients with meals.
甲型肝炎病毒（HAV）主要通过「粪-口途径」（Fecal–oral route）传播，即通过摄入被感染者粪便污染的水源、食物或冰块致病。最常见的传染源包括：摄入受生活污水污染海域捕捞的生食或半生贝类海鲜、食用未经充分清洗的生鲜蔬果，以及如厕后未彻底洗手的感染者加工制作的餐食。居住环境拥挤及卫生设施匮乏会急剧增加暴发风险。 支持工作者针对甲肝患者的感控要点： • 粪便污染防范规程：患有大便失禁、意识混乱或阿尔茨海默病的长者极易将粪便涂抹污染被褥、衣物、床档及轮椅扶手。支持工作者必须仔细检查照护环境，一旦发现粪便污染，立即使用含氯或医用消毒剂进行彻底清洁消毒；接触排泄物时必须穿戴一次性手套与防护隔离衣。 • 手部卫生硬门禁：处理便盆、便椅、马桶或污染衣物后，必须在流动水下用洗手液进行规范七步洗手；在参与餐食准备或协助长者进食前，必须再次严格洗手，坚决杜绝粪-口链条。

Hepatitis B (HBV): Bloodborne Transmission and Carrier State

乙型肝炎（HBV）：血液与体液传播途径及慢性携带危害
Hepatitis B virus (HBV) is spread through contact with infected blood and bodily fluids (including semen, vaginal secretions, and saliva containing blood). Modes of transmission include sharing contaminated needles or syringes, accidental sharps injuries among healthcare personnel, unprotected sexual intercourse, tattoos or body piercings with non-sterile equipment, sharing toothbrushes or razors, and vertical transmission from an infected mother to her baby during childbirth. Chronic HBV Infection: Many adults recover completely from HBV and develop protective immunity. However, some individuals—especially infants and young children—develop chronic persistent hepatitis B, becoming lifelong carriers. Chronic HBV infection frequently progresses over decades to chronic active hepatitis, liver cirrhosis (replacement of normal liver tissue with dense fibrous scar tissue), and hepatocellular carcinoma (liver cancer).
乙型肝炎病毒（HBV）主要通过接触受感染者的血液和高风险体液（精液、阴道分泌物及混有血液的唾液）进行传播。主要传播途径包括：共用被血液污染的注射针头、医护人员意外针刺伤害、无保护性接触、使用未经严格高压灭菌的器械进行文身或穿孔、共用剃须刀或牙刷，以及分娩过程中由受感染母亲垂直传染给新生儿。 乙肝慢性化与终身携带：绝大多数成年感染者在急性期后能够彻底清除病毒并产生终身保护性表面抗体；然而，一部分人群（尤其是新生儿及婴幼儿期感染者）极易转为「慢性持续性乙肝」，成为终身病毒携带者。慢性乙型肝炎若未规范抗病毒治疗，常在数十年内进展为肝硬化（正常肝小叶被纤维疤痕假小叶取代破坏）及原发性肝细胞癌（肝癌），对生命构成严重威胁。
PAGE 763 · 第 763 页 Hepatitis Prevention, Universal Precautions & Neonatal Care / 肝炎预防阻断、通用防护规程与新生儿垂直阻断

HEPATITIS B PROPHYLAXIS AND NEONATAL PROTECTION

乙型肝炎暴露后预防与母婴垂直传播阻断
In Canada, universal maternal screening for hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg) is standard clinical practice. To prevent vertical mother-to-child transmission, it is officially recommended that infants born to HBsAg-positive mothers receive a special post-exposure injection of Hepatitis B Immune Globulin (HBIG) immediately after birth, along with their first dose of the hepatitis B vaccine administered within 12 hours of delivery. Completing the full vaccine series provides over 95% protection against chronic carrier development.
在加拿大现行产科临床中，对所有孕妇进行乙肝表面抗原（HBsAg）常规产前筛查是一项普遍制度。为彻底切断母婴垂直传播链，国家免疫规程明确规定：HBsAg阳性母亲娩出的新生儿，出生后必须立即接受一次性乙型肝炎高效价免疫球蛋白（HBIG）肌肉注射，并在出生后12小时内接种第一剂乙肝重组疫苗。后续按月完成三剂全程接种，能为新生儿提供超过95%的保护率，阻断慢性携带发生。

Hepatitis C (HCV): Silent Epidemic and Modern Curative Antivirals

丙型肝炎（HCV）：隐匿进展与现代直接抗病毒药物根治
Hepatitis C virus (HCV) is a bloodborne pathogen spread primarily through percutaneous exposure to infected blood (such as sharing drug injection equipment, non-sterile tattoo/piercing needles, or historically through blood transfusions prior to universal blood screening in 1990). HCV can also be spread, though less efficiently, through sexual contact. The Silent Epidemic: Unlike HAV and HBV, acute HCV infection is often clinically silent (asymptomatic) or presents with mild flulike symptoms. Consequently, approximately 75% to 85% of people infected with HCV develop chronic infection without knowing it. Over 20 to 30 years, chronic HCV silently destroys liver architecture, leading to cirrhosis and end-stage liver disease. Fortunately, modern direct-acting antiviral (DAA) medications taken orally for 8 to 12 weeks now achieve sustained virological response (cure) rates exceeding 95%.
丙型肝炎病毒（HCV）是一种主要经由皮肤破损接触受污染血液传播的血源性病原体（常见诱因包括共用静脉注射针具、非灭菌文身穿孔器具，或在1990年血液全面筛查前接受输血制品）。性接触亦可传播，但传播效率较乙肝稍低。 隐匿性流行：与甲肝和乙肝不同，急性丙肝感染通常在临床上无明显症状（无黄疸隐匿性发病），或仅表现为轻微疲倦。因此，约75%至85%的感染者会在毫不知情的情况下悄然转变为慢性丙肝。在随后的20至30年间，慢性丙肝持续破坏肝细胞微结构，最终引发不可逆肝硬化与肝功能衰竭。值得庆幸的是，现代口服直接抗病毒药物（DAA）连续治疗8至12周，已能实现超过95%的病毒学完全根除治愈率。

Hepatitis D (HDV) and Hepatitis E (HEV)

丁型肝炎（HDV）与戊型肝炎（HEV）特征
• Hepatitis D (HDV / Delta virus): A defective RNA virus that requires the presence of Hepatitis B virus (HBV) to replicate. It only infects individuals who are already chronically infected with HBV (superinfection) or infected simultaneously with HBV (coinfection). HDV dramatically accelerates the progression to severe liver cirrhosis. • Hepatitis E (HEV): Similar to Hepatitis A, HEV is transmitted predominantly via the fecal–oral route through contaminated drinking water. While typically self-limiting, HEV carries a mortality rate of up to 20% to 25% among pregnant women in their third trimester due to fulminant acute liver failure.
• 丁型肝炎（HDV / Delta 病毒）：一种缺陷型RNA病毒，必须依赖乙肝病毒（HBV）表面抗原提供的外壳才能在人体内组装复制。它只发生于慢性乙肝病毒携带者（重叠感染）或与乙肝同时感染的患者（联合感染）。丁肝病毒的叠加会急剧加速肝硬化与暴发性肝坏死的病程。 • 戊型肝炎（HEV）：其传播机制与甲肝类似，主要经由粪-口途径摄入受污染饮用水致病，多见于供水设施落后地区。大多数普通患者呈自限性恢复，但妊娠晚期孕妇一旦感染戊肝，暴发性肝衰竭病死率可高达20%至25%。

SUPPORT WORKER SAFETY PROTOCOLS: UNIVERSAL PRECAUTIONS FOR HEPATITIS CARE / 支持工作者执业安全红线：针对各型肝炎的通用防护与锐器管理

PAGE 764 · 第 764 页 Sexually Transmitted Infections (STIs) & Genital Herpes / 性传播感染（STIs）概述与生殖器疱疹病理

SEXUALLY TRANSMITTED INFECTIONS (STIS)

性传播感染（STIs）：流行病学与传播机制
Sexually transmitted infections (STIs)—also referred to historically as sexually transmitted diseases (STDs) or venereal diseases (VD)—are infectious diseases transmitted primarily through sexual contact (vaginal, anal, or oral sex). Some STIs can also be transmitted from mother to infant during pregnancy or childbirth, through infected blood products, or by sharing contaminated needles. Common STIs in Canada include genital herpes, chlamydia, gonorrhea, syphilis, human papillomavirus (HPV), and trichomoniasis.
性传播感染（Sexually Transmitted Infections，STIs）——在传统医学上亦被称为性病（STDs）或花柳病（VD）——是指主要经由阴道性交、肛交或口腔性行为等性接触途径传播的一组感染性疾患。部分性传播病原体还可经由受感染母亲在孕期经胎盘或分娩期垂直传染给新生儿、通过输入受污染血制品传播，或因共用注射针头传播。加拿大高发的常见STIs包括生殖器疱疹、衣原体感染、淋病、梅毒、人乳头瘤病毒（HPV）感染及滴虫病。

Genital Herpes (HSV-1 & HSV-2)

生殖器疱疹（单纯疱疹病毒1型与2型）
Genital herpes is a viral infection caused by the herpes simplex virus (HSV). There are two types: HSV-1 (which traditionally causes oral herpes / cold sores on the lips, but is increasingly responsible for genital lesions due to oral-genital contact) and HSV-2 (which primarily causes genital herpes). An infected person can shed the virus even when no sores or active blisters are present (asymptomatic viral shedding). If spread to a partner, symptoms typically appear 3 days to 1 week after exposure. Initial outbreaks present with painful, fluid-filled vesicles around the genitals or rectum that rupture into tender ulcerations, accompanied by fever, swollen inguinal lymph nodes, and dysuria. The virus remains dormant in sensory nerve ganglia for life, periodically reactivating during periods of physical stress, illness, or fatigue. Antiviral medications (such as acyclovir) lessen the severity and frequency of outbreaks but do not cure the virus.
生殖器疱疹是由单纯疱疹病毒（HSV）引起的慢性复发性病毒性皮肤黏膜病变。该病毒分为两型：HSV-1型（传统上常引起口唇疱疹/单纯冷疮，但因口-生殖器性接触日益成为生殖器病变的重要病因）与HSV-2型（原发生殖器疱疹的主要致病型别）。极为关键的是：即便在局部完全无可见水疱或溃疡的无症状期，感染者生殖道黏膜依然能向外界排毒（无症状病毒脱落），并具极高传染性！初次感染潜伏期一般为接触后3天至1周。典型初发表现为外阴、阴茎或肛周区域群集性、充满液体的灼痛性小水疱，破溃后形成糜烂与疼痛浅溃疡，常伴随发热、腹股沟淋巴结肿痛及排尿烧灼痛。疱疹痊愈后病毒沿感觉神经纤维逆行潜伏于骶神经节终生存在，在疲劳、抵抗力低下、创伤或应激时反复激活复发。阿昔洛韦等抗病毒药物能显著减轻发作严重度并缩短病程，但尚无法彻底根除神经节内的潜伏病毒。
FIGURE 33.1: Genital Herpes (Herpes Simplex Virus Lesions)
图 33.1：生殖器疱疹典型病损（单纯疱疹病毒水疱与浅表溃疡）
A, Genital herpes sores on the penis.
A：男性阴茎包皮及冠状沟处多发性痛性生殖器疱疹溃疡。
B, Genital herpes sores on the perineum.
B：女性会阴及大阴唇部位破溃糜烂的生殖器疱疹病损。
PAGE 765 · 第 765 页 Common STIs: Chlamydia, Gonorrhea, Syphilis, HPV & Trichomoniasis / 常见性传播感染：衣原体、淋病、梅毒、HPV与滴虫病

CLINICAL PROFILE OF COMMON STIS

常见性传播感染的病原学特征与临床表现
• Chlamydia (Chlamydia trachomatis): The most commonly reported bacterial STI in Canada, especially among adolescents and young adults. It is known as the 'silent infection' because up to 70% of women and 50% of men have no symptoms. When symptoms occur, they include abnormal discharge, burning on urination, and lower abdominal pain. Untreated chlamydia can ascend into the upper reproductive tract, causing Pelvic Inflammatory Disease (PID), chronic pelvic pain, ectopic pregnancy, and permanent infertility in women. • Gonorrhea (Neisseria gonorrhoeae): A bacterial infection causing yellowish-green purulent discharge from the urethra or vagina, painful burning urination, and testicular swelling. It can also infect the pharynx, rectum, and eyes. Untreated gonorrhea can cause PID, epididymitis, and disseminated systemic infections.
• 衣原体感染（Chlamydia）：由沙眼衣原体引起，是加拿大报告病例数最多的细菌性性病，在青少年与年轻活跃人群中尤为高发。该病被称为「隐匿性感染」，高达70%的女性及50%的男性感染后完全没有任何自主症状。有症状者常表现为生殖道异常分泌物增多、排尿刺痛及下腹坠胀。未经彻底治疗的衣原体可上行侵犯子宫和输卵管，导致严重的盆腔炎性疾病（PID）、慢性盆腔顽固痛、异位妊娠（宫外孕）以及永久性不孕不育症。 • 淋病（Gonorrhea）：由淋病奈瑟菌引起的化脓性尿道生殖黏膜感染。男性典型表现为尿道口红肿并流出大量黄绿色脓性稠厚分泌物、剧烈排尿痛；女性可有阴道脓性白带及排尿烧灼感。淋球菌还可感染咽喉部、直肠黏膜及新生儿结膜（淋菌性眼炎）。未经治疗者可并发附睾炎、输卵管闭塞及播散性淋球菌关节炎败血症。
• Syphilis (Treponema pallidum): A complex bacterial spirochete infection progressing through distinct clinical stages: - Primary Syphilis: A single, painless indurated sore (chancre) appears at the site of entry (genitals, anus, or lips) and heals spontaneously after 3 to 6 weeks, misleading clients into believing they are cured. - Secondary Syphilis: Weeks later, generalized non-itchy rash appears on the body, prominently on the palms of the hands and soles of the feet, accompanied by fever, swollen lymph nodes, and patchy hair loss (alopecia). - Latent & Tertiary Syphilis: If untreated, the bacterium enters a silent latent phase lasting years, eventually progressing to tertiary syphilis, which attacks the cardiovascular system (aortic aneurysms) and central nervous system (neurosyphilis, causing paralysis, dementia, and blindness). • Human Papillomavirus (HPV): A widespread viral infection. Low-risk strains cause genital warts (condylomata acuminata), while high-risk oncogenic strains (such as HPV 16 and 18) are directly responsible for over 90% of cervical cancers, as well as anal, penile, and oropharyngeal cancers. The HPV vaccine (Gardasil 9) is routinely offered to school-aged children in Canada to prevent infection.
• 梅毒（Syphilis）：由苍白螺旋体引起的全身多阶段慢性感染： - 一期梅毒：在病原体侵入部位（外生殖器、肛周或口唇）出现单个硬韧无痛性圆形溃疡（硬下疳 Chancre）。由于硬下疳不痛且在3至6周内自行愈合消退，极易使感染者误以为病已自愈而延误就医。 - 二期梅毒：硬下疳消退数周后，螺旋体随血液播散至全身，典型特征为手掌和足底出现弥漫性铜红色无痒性斑丘疹，伴低热、全身淋巴结无痛性肿大及虫蚀状脱发。 - 潜伏期与三期梅毒：未经足量青霉素治疗者进入数年至数十年的无症状潜伏期，最终进展为破坏性三期晚期梅毒，侵犯心血管系统（梅毒性主动脉瘤破裂）及中枢神经系统（神经梅毒引起共济失调、偏瘫、梅毒性痴呆与失明）。 • 人乳头瘤病毒（HPV）：最普遍的性传播病毒之一。低危型引起生殖器尖锐湿疣（菜花样赘生物）；而高危致癌型别（如HPV-16和18型）与90%以上的宫颈癌、肛门癌、阴茎癌及口咽癌的发生具有直接因果关联。加拿大各省公立学校常规为适龄学童免费提供九价HPV疫苗，以从源头阻断相关恶性肿瘤。
PAGE 766 · 第 766 页 Safe Sex Practices & Cancer (Oncology) Introduction / 安全性行为干预原则与恶性肿瘤（癌症）病理概论

PREVENTING STI TRANSMISSION & CLIENT EDUCATION

预防性传播感染的阻断屏障与知情同意规范
To prevent the transmission of STIs and HIV, healthcare professionals advocate consistent, correct barrier methods. Clients must be advised to use a latex or polyurethane condom or dental dam with every sexual activity, even when another form of contraception (such as birth control pills or IUDs) is being used. Barrier methods provide the primary physical barrier against mucosal pathogens. Persons testing positive for an STI must abstain from sexual contact until curative treatment is completed, notify all recent sexual partners so they can be tested and treated simultaneously (preventing reinfection 'ping-pong' cycles), and complete all prescribed medications exactly as directed.
为彻底阻断性传播感染与艾滋病毒的传播，现代医学极力倡导全程规范使用物理屏障防护。必须指导服务对象在每次进行性接触时，全程正确使用乳胶或聚氨酯安全套（Condom）或口交膜（Dental dam）——即便女方已采取口服避孕药或宫内节育器等避孕措施，安全套依然是阻断黏膜病原体交换必不可少的物理防线。确诊STIs的患者在完成全部疗程并经复查转阴前必须严禁性生活；务必通知近期所有性伴侣同步接受筛查与治疗，以切断相互乒乓传染的死循环；并严格遵医嘱足量足疗程服完所有抗菌药物。

CANCER: TUMOUR PATHOLOGY AND CELLULAR TRANSFORMATION

癌症：肿瘤病理学分类与细胞恶性转化
Cancer is not a single disease, but a broad group of more than 200 distinct diseases characterized by out-of-control cell division, unregulated growth, and abnormal cellular morphology that can originate in almost any tissue or organ of the body. Normal cells divide and reproduce in a tightly controlled cycle to replace old or damaged cells. In cancer, genetic mutations override normal growth checkpoints, causing cells to proliferate uncontrollably, forming an abnormal tissue mass known as a tumour (neoplasm).
癌症（Cancer）绝非单一疾病，而是涵盖200多种具有失控性细胞分裂、自主异常增殖以及细胞结构去分化形态学改变的异质性疾病总称，几乎可原发于人体任何组织器官。在正常生理状态下，人体细胞的分裂与更新受到细胞周期蛋白及抑癌基因的严密调控，仅在替换老化受损细胞时有序增生。在癌变过程中，原癌基因突变激活或抑癌基因失活，打破了这一动态平衡，导致细胞无节制疯狂增生累积，形成称为「肿瘤」（Tumour / Neoplasm）的异常病理团块。

Tumour Classification: Benign versus Malignant

肿瘤的两大分类：良性肿瘤与恶性肿瘤鉴别
Tumours are categorized into two fundamental pathological classes: 1. Benign Tumours: Noncancerous growths. Benign tumour cells closely resemble their parent tissue, grow slowly, are typically surrounded by a fibrous capsule, and do not invade adjacent normal tissue. They do not metastasize to distant body parts. While usually not life-threatening, benign tumours can cause serious complications if they grow in enclosed spaces, such as within the skull (compressing delicate brain tissue), or when they press on vital blood vessels and nerves. 2. Malignant Tumours: Cancerous growths. Malignant cells are poorly differentiated (atypical), divide rapidly, lack contact inhibition, and aggressively invade and infiltrate surrounding healthy structures. If left untreated, malignant tumours enter blood and lymphatic vessels, travel to remote organs (metastasis), destroy normal organ function, and result in death.
根据组织病理学生物学行为，肿瘤分为两大本质不同的基本类别： 1. 良性肿瘤（Benign Tumours）：非癌性新生物。良性肿瘤细胞分化良好，形态与来源正常母体细胞高度相似；生长缓慢，周围通常有一层完整的纤维包膜包裹，具有膨胀性生长的特征；绝不侵润浸润周边正常组织，亦绝不发生远处转移。良性肿瘤切除后极少复发，通常不危及生命。然而，若良性肿瘤生长在颅腔等封闭密闭骨性腔隙内（持续压迫生命中枢脑组织），或压迫重要大血管与神经束时，亦可引起严重的致残性功能障碍。 2. 恶性肿瘤（Malignant Tumours）：即通俗所谓的「癌症」。恶性细胞分化不良（异型性显著）、分裂增殖极快；具有浸润性破坏周边正常组织结构的恶性生物学特征；若未及时干预，恶性细胞可脱落侵入淋巴管和微血管，随体循环播散至远隔脏器定植生长（转移 Metastasis），摧毁关键脏器功能，最终导致机体衰竭死亡。

FIGURE 33.2: Benign versus malignant tumours. A, Benign tumours grow within a localized encapsulated area. B, Malignant tumours invade adjacent tissues and can spread into blood vessels.

图 33.2：良性肿瘤与恶性肿瘤的浸润行为对比。A：良性肿瘤局限于包膜内膨胀性生长，不侵蚀邻近组织。B：恶性肿瘤突破基底膜向周边健康组织浸润性侵袭，并可穿透毛细血管和淋巴管向远处转移。

PAGE 767 · 第 767 页 Metastasis Mechanisms, Risk Factors & Cancer Treatments / 癌症转移途径、高危致癌因素与肿瘤综合治疗模式

METASTASIS: THE SPREAD OF MALIGNANT CELLS

肿瘤转移（Metastasis）：恶性细胞远处播散机制
Metastasis is the spread of cancer from its primary site of origin to secondary, distant parts of the body. Cancer cells secrete proteolytic enzymes that degrade the extracellular matrix, break off from the primary tumour, and intravasate into regional lymphatic channels or blood capillaries. The circulating tumour cells are transported through the bloodstream, lodge in capillary beds of target organs (such as the lungs, liver, bones, or brain), extravasate into the tissue, and induce new blood vessel growth (angiogenesis) to establish secondary metastatic tumours.
转移（Metastasis）是指恶性肿瘤细胞从原发部位脱落，经过各种途径播散到身体远隔器官并继续增殖形成同类型新肿瘤的过程。恶性细胞能分泌基质金属蛋白酶等溶解酶破坏细胞间基质，脱落并穿透毛细血管或淋巴管内皮进入循环系统（肿瘤血管浸润）。游离的癌细胞随血流或淋巴液运行，最终滞留在特定靶器官（如肺、肝脏、骨骼及中枢脑组织）的微血管床中，穿出血管壁侵入靶组织，并通过释放血管内皮生长因子诱导新生血管形成（肿瘤血管新生），建立新的继发转移性肿瘤病灶。
FIGURE 33.3: Metastasis of Cancer Cells
图 33.3：恶性肿瘤的远处转移机制示意
A, Primary tumour located in the lung tissue.
A：肺组织内的原发恶性肿瘤实性病灶。
B, The tumour has metastasized via circulation to the contralateral lung and mediastinal lymph nodes.
B：肿瘤细胞经血液和淋巴系统播散转移至对侧肺野及纵隔淋巴结。

Risk Factors and Warning Signs of Cancer (CAUTION)

高危致癌因素与癌症七大早期警示体征（CAUTION法则）
Major carcinogenic risk factors include: tobacco use (the leading preventable cause of lung, bladder, and throat cancer), exposure to ionizing and ultraviolet (UV) radiation (sunlight, tanning beds), exposure to industrial chemical carcinogens (asbestos, benzene, radon), dietary factors (high fat, low fibre, processed meats), alcohol consumption, certain hormones (estrogen replacement), and viral oncogenes (HPV, Hepatitis B and C, Epstein-Barr virus). The Canadian Cancer Society promotes the 'CAUTION' mnemonic for early warning signs: • C: Change in bowel or bladder habits (persistent diarrhea, constipation, hematuria) • A: A sore that does not heal (especially on skin, mouth, or tongue) • U: Unusual bleeding or discharge from any body orifice (rectal bleeding, postmenopausal vaginal bleeding) • T: Thickening or lump in the breast, testicles, or elsewhere • I: Indigestion or difficulty swallowing (persistent dysphagia) • O: Obvious change in a wart or mole (asymmetry, border irregularity, colour changes) • N: Nagging cough or persistent hoarseness
公认的主要致癌高危因素包括：烟草使用（肺癌、喉癌及膀胱癌的首要可预防致病因）、电离辐射与过量紫外线（UV）暴晒（日晒、紫外线晒黑舱诱发皮肤癌）、工业化学致癌物职业暴露（石棉引起间皮瘤、苯致白血病、氡气）、不良膳食结构（高脂肪、低纤维、大量摄入加工腌制红肉）、长期酗酒、激素水平失衡（长期雌激素替代）以及致癌病毒感染（HPV诱发宫颈癌、HBV/HCV诱发肝癌、EB病毒诱发鼻咽癌）。 加拿大癌症协会确立了著名的「CAUTION」癌症早期警示体征速记法则： • C（Change）：排便或排尿习惯的顽固性改变（不明原因的长期腹泻、便秘、无痛血尿） • A（A sore）：经久不愈的皮肤、口腔或黏膜浅表溃疡创口 • U（Unusual bleeding）：任何生理腔道出现异常出血或不正常分泌物（便血、绝经后阴道出血、咳血） • T（Thickening or lump）：乳腺、睾丸、颈部或全身任何部位触及无痛性硬块或局部增厚 • I（Indigestion）：长期消化不良或进行性吞咽困难（食管下段噎堵感） • O（Obvious change）：原有痣或疣的形状、颜色或大小发生明显改变（不对称、边缘锯齿、杂色） • N（Nagging cough）：持续顽固性干咳或久治不愈的声音嘶哑

Cancer Treatment Modalities

癌症的三大现代临床治疗模式
Cancer treatments are customized based on tumour staging (TNM classification), histology, client age, and general physical condition: 1. Surgery: The physical surgical excision of the primary tumour and regional lymph nodes before metastasis occurs. Can be curative, diagnostic (biopsy), or palliative (relieving obstruction, bleeding, or pain). 2. Radiation Therapy (Radiotherapy): Utilizes high-energy ionizing X-rays or gamma rays to damage cancer cell DNA, destroying their capacity to divide while sparing as much healthy tissue as possible. Can be administered externally (external beam radiation) or internally via implanted radioactive seeds (brachytherapy). 3. Chemotherapy: The administration of potent antineoplastic medications intravenously or orally that travel throughout the entire systemic circulation to kill rapidly dividing malignant cells. Because chemotherapy acts systemically, it also affects healthy rapidly dividing cells, particularly bone marrow, hair follicles, and digestive tract mucosa.
现代肿瘤学根据病理分期（TNM分期）、组织学分型、患者年龄及体能评分制定多学科综合治疗方案： 1. 外科手术（Surgery）：在肿瘤发生广泛远处转移前，通过手术完整切除原发病灶及引流区淋巴结。可为根治性手术、诊断性活检（Biopsy）或姑息性手术（解除肠梗阻、压迫或压迫性止痛）。 2. 放射治疗（Radiation Therapy / 放疗）：利用高能X射线、伽马射线或质子束等电离辐射精确照射肿瘤靶区，破坏恶性细胞的DNA双螺旋链使其丧失分裂能力，同时最大程度保护邻近正常器官。分为体外照射（外放疗）与放射性粒子瘤内植入（近距离组织间插植放疗）。 3. 化学药物治疗（Chemotherapy / 化疗）：通过静脉滴注或口服强效抗肿瘤化学药物，使其随体循环分布全身，杀灭微小转移灶及快速增殖的癌细胞。由于化疗药物缺乏完全的靶向选择性，在杀灭癌细胞的同时亦不可避免地杀伤机体正常快速分裂的组织细胞，尤其累及骨髓造血干细胞、毛囊上皮细胞及消化道胃肠黏膜。
PAGE 768 · 第 768 页 PSW Cancer Care Priorities, Side Effects & Skin Cancers / 支持工作者癌症综合照护要点、治疗副反应应对与皮肤癌鉴别

SUPPORT WORKER CLINICAL PRIORITIES FOR CLIENTS WITH CANCER

支持工作者针对癌症患者的核心照护临床重点
Providing compassionate, vigilant care for clients undergoing cancer treatment requires holistic attention to physical symptoms, treatment side effects, and deep psychological distress: • Fluid and Nutritional Support: Cancer cachexia (wasting syndrome) and chemotherapy-induced nausea/anorexia compromise nutrition. Provide small, frequent, nutrient-dense meals; encourage high-calorie shakes; offer client preferences; maintain hydration. • Skin Integrity & Pressure Injury Prevention: Frail, bedridden cancer clients are at extreme risk for skin breakdown. Reposition at least every 2 hours; inspect bony prominences; apply skin moisturizers; maintain dry, wrinkle-free bed linens. • Bowel Elimination Monitoring: Opioid analgesics prescribed for severe cancer pain routinely cause severe constipation; chemotherapy may cause debilitating diarrhea. Document bowel movements accurately and notify the nurse if constipation or diarrhea occurs. • Fatigue Management & Rest Periods: Cancer-related fatigue is severe and persistent. Schedule essential ADLs around the client's peak energy windows, and provide uninterrupted rest periods. • Pain Assessment & Emotional Validation: Never ignore cancer pain. Promptly report nonverbal pain cues (grimacing, moaning, restlessness) to the nurse so analgesics can be adjusted. Provide a calm, validating presence for anxiety and fear.
照护接受肿瘤治疗的服务对象需要兼顾躯体症状管理、放化疗严重副作用防护以及深层心理危机支持： • 补充体液与高能量营养：癌症恶病质（消瘦衰竭）以及化疗引起的剧烈恶心和食欲不振会严重损害营养状态。提供少量多餐、高蛋白、高营养密度的餐食；准备营养补充奶昔；优先满足长者饮食偏好并保证充足饮水量。 • 维护皮肤完整性与防压疮：消瘦卧床患者极易发生组织缺血坏死。至少每2小时协助翻身变换体位一次；严密检查骨隆突部位皮肤有无发红压痕；涂抹医用润肤乳；保持床单干燥、平整无褶皱。 • 严密监测肠道排泄：用于晚期癌痛控制的强阿片类麻醉镇痛药（如吗啡、芬太尼）极易导致严重麻痹性便秘；而化疗药物则常诱发腹泻脱水。如实准确记录每日大便频次与性状，出现便秘或腹泻立即向主管护士汇报。 • 疲劳管理与体能蓄积：癌性疲乏具有深度与不可逆性。协助患者在精力最好的时段完成洗漱等核心活动，并为其提供绝对安静的日间平卧休息时段。 • 疼痛评估与情感共情赋能：绝不可忽视癌症疼痛。密切观察非言语疼痛表征（皱眉、呻吟、烦躁不安、抗拒翻身），及时汇报护士以便按阶梯调整止痛方案。面对恐惧与焦虑，给予稳定、温和而坚定的情感陪伴。

Managing Treatment Adverse Effects: Alopecia & Stomatitis

化疗常见不良反应应对：脱发与口腔炎的专业护理
• Alopecia (Hair Loss): Chemotherapy damages hair follicle matrix cells, resulting in partial or complete hair loss across the scalp and body. Hair loss profoundly affects self-image and self-esteem. The support worker should handle remaining hair gently with wide-toothed combs, respect the client's choice to wear attractive headscarves, turbans, hats, or wigs, and reassure them that hair almost always regrows after chemotherapy ends. • Stomatitis & Oral Care: Chemo- and radiation therapy damage mucosal linings, causing painful oral ulcers (stomatitis / oral mucositis). Provide gentle oral hygiene after meals and at bedtime using a soft sponge-tipped swab (toothette) or ultra-soft toothbrush. Avoid alcohol-based commercial mouthwashes, which cause severe burning; use warm saline or physician-ordered bland rinses. Offer soft, non-acidic, lukewarm foods.
• 脱发（Alopecia）：化疗药物杀伤毛囊基质生发细胞，常导致头发大面积脱落甚至全身毛发全脱。脱发对患者的自我形象与心理尊严打击巨大。支持工作者应用宽齿梳极其轻柔地梳理残留头发；全力支持长者佩戴假发、美观头巾、帽子或软帽的选择；耐心地向其保证：在化疗疗程完全结束后，头发绝大多数均会重新生长。 • 口腔黏膜炎（Stomatitis）：放化疗破坏消化道黏膜上皮再生，引发口腔内多发性剧痛性糜烂与溃疡。每餐后及睡前使用超软毛牙刷或医用海绵口腔拭子轻柔清洁口腔；严禁使用含酒精的市售漱口水（可引发剧烈烧灼刺痛），应使用医嘱规定的温生理盐水或温和漱口液漱口；提供温凉、软质无刺激性食物。

FIGURE 33.4: Common skin cancers. A and B, Two common clinical presentations of basal cell carcinoma (pearly nodule with central depression). C, Squamous cell carcinoma (firm red nodule with scaling). D, Malignant melanoma showing irregular asymmetric borders and varied pigmentation.

图 33.4：常见皮肤恶性肿瘤临床鉴别。A和B：基底细胞癌的两种典型表现（半透明珍珠样结节伴中央凹陷性溃疡）。C：鳞状细胞癌（坚硬浸润性红色结节伴表面角化脱屑）。D：恶性黑色素瘤（形态严重不对称、边缘锯齿状侵蚀并呈现斑驳黑褐杂色）。

PAGE 769 · 第 769 页 Skin Cancers & Introduction to Common Skin Disorders / 皮肤恶性肿瘤鉴别与常见皮肤系统疾患总览

SKIN CANCER: BASAL CELL, SQUAMOUS CELL & MALIGNANT MELANOMA

皮肤癌三大类型：基底细胞癌、鳞状细胞癌与恶性黑色素瘤
Skin cancer is the most common of all cancers in Canada, strongly linked to ultraviolet (UV) radiation from sun exposure and tanning equipment. There are three primary types: 1. Basal Cell Carcinoma (BCC): Arises in the basal layer of the epidermis. It is the most common form (over 70% of skin cancers). BCC appears as a smooth, pearly, translucent bump or firm flesh-coloured nodule, often with visible tiny blood vessels (telangiectasias). It grows slowly, rarely metastasizes, but can invade local tissues if untreated. 2. Squamous Cell Carcinoma (SCC): Arises in squamous keratinocytes. Characterized by a firm, rough, scaly, or crusty red nodule that may ulcerate. While typically localized, SCC can metastasize to regional lymph nodes if ignored. 3. Malignant Melanoma: Arises in pigment-producing melanocytes. Although representing less than 5% of skin cancers, melanoma is by far the most lethal because it aggressively invades blood and lymphatic systems, metastasizing rapidly to internal organs (lungs, liver, brain).
皮肤癌是加拿大全境发病率最高的恶性肿瘤，其发生与过量接受日光紫外线（UV）辐射及室内紫外线晒黑舱具有极为明确的直接因果关联。临床最主要的三大类型为： 1. 基底细胞癌（BCC）：原发于表皮基底层细胞，占皮肤癌总数的70%以上，属于最常见的皮肤癌。病损典型表现为光滑、珍珠般微亮半透明结节或硬实质结节，表面常可见毛细血管扩张伴中央轻度溃陷。该型生长极其缓慢，极罕见发生远处转移，但若长期忽视可破坏侵蚀深部软组织。 2. 鳞状细胞癌（SCC）：原发于表皮棘层角质形成细胞。表现为质地坚硬、表面粗糙不平、覆有鳞屑或污褐色厚痂的暗红色浸润结节，中心易破溃出血。多数局限，但若未获早期治疗，可转移至区域引流淋巴结。 3. 恶性黑色素瘤（Malignant Melanoma）：原发于产生黑色素的黑色素细胞。虽然在皮肤癌中占比不足5%，但其侵袭性极强、恶性度极高，早期即可穿透真皮毛细血管发生全身淋巴与血行远处播散（转移至肺、肝、脑），是致死率最高的最凶险皮肤癌。

ABCDE Assessment Criteria for Moles and Skin Lesions

黑色素瘤临床鉴别之 ABCDE 评估法则
Support workers providing bathing and personal care must observe the client's skin and report any suspicious mole changes using the ABCDE criteria: • A - Asymmetry: One half of the mole does not match the other half. • B - Border: Edges are irregular, ragged, notched, or blurred. • C - Colour: The pigmentation is not uniform; exhibits shades of tan, brown, black, with patches of red, white, or blue. • D - Diameter: Greater than 6 millimetres (the size of a pencil eraser), although melanomas can be smaller. • E - Evolving: The mole is changing in size, shape, elevation, or bleeding/itching.
支持工作者在协助长者沐浴及更衣过程中，应注意观察其体表痣与色素斑变化，掌握经典的 ABCDE 黑色素瘤临床鉴别标准： • A（Asymmetry 对称性）：病变部位左右或上下两侧不对称，一侧形态与另一侧完全不吻合。 • B（Border 边缘）：边缘参差不齐、呈锯齿状、切迹凹凸或界限模糊不清。 • C（Colour 颜色）：颜色杂乱不均一，同一斑块内杂糅深棕、炭黑、暗红甚至灰白或蓝黑色。 • D（Diameter 直径）：直径通常大于6毫米（超过普通铅笔末端橡皮擦大小），但早期黑色素瘤亦可较小。 • E（Evolving 进展变化）：色素痣在近期内发生显著变化，包括体积迅速增大、凸起增厚、瘙痒破溃或自发性出血渗液。

COMMON DISORDERS OF THE INTEGUMENTARY SYSTEM

常见皮肤及皮下组织疾患概述
The skin (integument) is the body's first line of defense against infection, dehydration, and environmental injury. Common dermatological conditions seen in clinical support work include fungal infections (athlete's foot), deep bacterial infections of hair follicles (furuncles/boils), encapsulated lesions (cysts), and hypersensitivity allergic reactions (contact dermatitis).
皮肤（Integumentary system）是机体抵御微生物侵袭、防止水分过度蒸发丢失并抵御外界物理化学损伤的第一道天然生理屏障。在临床日常支持工作中，经常遇到的皮肤疾患包括真菌感染（足癣）、毛囊深部细菌性化脓（疖/痈）、上皮包裹性闭合腔隙（囊肿）以及接触致敏原引发的超敏反应（接触性皮炎）。
PAGE 770 · 第 770 页 Figure 33.5 Part 1: Common Skin Disorders (A–F) / 图 33.5 常见皮肤病全图谱（第1部分：A至F）

FIGURE 33.5: COMMON SKIN DISORDERS (PART 1, IMAGES A TO F)

图 33.5：常见皮肤系统疾患全图解（第 1 部分：图 A 至 图 F）
FIGURE 33.5 (Part 1): Authentic Clinical Presentations A through F
图 33.5（前 6 幅）：足癣、疖、囊肿、接触性皮炎、湿疹与脓疱疮原版图谱
A, Athlete’s foot (Tinea pedis): Fungal infection causing maceration, peeling, and redness between toes.
A：足癣（脚气）：致病真菌感染引发的趾缝浸渍、脱皮撕裂与潮红发痒。
B, Boils (Furuncles): Deep, painful, pus-filled staphylococcal infection of a hair follicle.
B：疖（Furuncles）：毛囊深部急性化脓性葡萄球菌感染，呈红肿热痛坚实结节伴中央化脓坏死。
C, Cyst: Closed, abnormal sac with distinct membrane containing fluid or semi-solid keratinous material.
C：囊肿（Cyst）：具有完整囊壁膜的异常闭合性囊腔，内含浆液或豆腐渣样角质皮脂半固体。
D, Contact dermatitis: Pruritic erythema and vesicular eruption caused by direct contact with allergens.
D：接触性皮炎：皮肤直接接触刺激物或过敏原后诱发的急性红斑水肿与水疱反应。
E, Eczema (Atopic dermatitis): Chronic, intensely itchy, scaly, and excoriated plaques.
E：湿疹（特应性皮炎）：慢性反复发作的剧烈瘙痒性浸润苔藓化红斑与抓痕鳞屑。
F, Impetigo: Highly contagious superficial bacterial infection featuring honey-coloured crusts.
F：脓疱疮（黄水疮）：高度接触传染性细菌性皮肤病，特征为破溃后形成的典型蜜黄色结痂。
PAGE 771 · 第 771 页 Figure 33.5 Part 2: Common Skin Disorders (G–L) / 图 33.5 常见皮肤病全图谱（第2部分：G至L）

FIGURE 33.5: COMMON SKIN DISORDERS (PART 2, IMAGES G TO L)

图 33.5：常见皮肤系统疾患全图解（第 2 部分：图 G 至 图 L）
FIGURE 33.5 (Part 2): Authentic Clinical Presentations G through L
图 33.5（后 6 幅）：银屑病、疥疮、脂溢性角化、带状疱疹、皮赘与荨麻疹原版图谱
G, Psoriasis: Autoimmune plaque disorder characterized by circumscribed silvery-white scaling plaques.
G：银屑病（牛皮癣）：自身免疫介导的慢性斑块病变，表面覆有多层干燥银白色云母状鳞屑。
H, Scabies: Intensely itchy parasitic infestation caused by Sarcoptes scabiei burrowing under skin.
H：疥疮（Scabies）：人疥螨侵入表皮角质层挖掘微小隧道产卵引起的剧烈夜间奇痒性丘疹。
I, Seborrheic keratosis: Benign, noncancerous warty skin growths with a 'stuck-on' appearance.
I：脂溢性角化病（老年斑）：良性非癌性上皮增生性斑块，表面粗糙呈蜡状附着外观。
J, Shingles (Herpes zoster): Reactivated viral eruption forming painful vesicles along a thoracic nerve path.
J：带状疱疹（缠腰龙）：水痘病毒复发，沿单侧胸神经节支配区呈带状排列的剧痛水疱群。
K, Skin tags (Acrochordons): Soft, small, benign pedunculated growths common in friction skinfolds.
K：皮赘（软纤维瘤）：多见于颈周及腋窝等皮肤摩擦褶皱处的柔软有蒂良性肉赘。
L, Urticaria (Hives): Allergic cutaneous reaction presenting with transient, raised, edematous wheals.
L：荨麻疹（风团）：肥大细胞释放组胺导致的急性过敏性水肿性扁平红斑风团与局部红晕。
PAGE 772 · 第 772 页 Dermatological Care: Eczema, Impetigo, Psoriasis, Scabies & Scleroderma / 皮肤疾患专科护理：湿疹、脓疱疮、银屑病、疥疮与硬皮病

CLINICAL MANAGEMENT OF COMMON DERMATOLOGICAL CONDITIONS

常见皮肤疾病的临床护理与传播控制规程
• Eczema (Atopic Dermatitis): Characterized by dry, red, severely pruritic skin with papular or vesicular lesions that weep and crust over. When caring for clients with eczema: maintain room humidity; bathe with lukewarm water and mild soap-free cleansers; avoid long hot baths; pat skin dry gently without rubbing; apply thick emollient ointments immediately after bathing (while skin is still damp) to lock in hydration; keep client fingernails trimmed short to prevent excoriation and secondary bacterial infection. • Impetigo: Highly contagious bacterial infection caused by Staphylococcus or Streptococcus, presenting with itchy, fragile blisters around the mouth, nose, and hands that rupture to form honey-coloured crusts. Care: Wash crusts gently with warm soapy water; apply prescribed topical antibacterial ointment; practice strict contact precautions; isolate the client's towels, linens, and clothing and launder in hot water.
• 湿疹（特应性皮炎）：表现为皮肤重度干燥、剧烈奇痒，常伴有丘疹、丘疱疹、渗液及结痂，反复抓挠可致局部皮肤苔藓样增厚。照护要点：保持居室适宜温湿度；使用温水及不含皂基的温和清洁乳洗浴，严禁长时间烫热水澡；浴后用柔软毛巾轻轻蘸干而切勿用力来回擦磨；在皮肤尚微湿时立即厚涂医用凡士林或屏障修复润肤软膏锁住水分；修剪磨平指甲，防范抓破诱发继发细菌感染。 • 脓疱疮（黄水疮）：由金黄色葡萄球菌或化脓性链球菌引起的高传染性浅表脓痂疹，多见于口周、鼻周及四肢。护理要点：使用温肥皂水轻柔敷软并清洗脓痂；涂抹医嘱抗菌药膏；严格执行接触隔离；专人专用毛巾与被褥，衣物单洗并在高温热水中消毒洗涤。
• Psoriasis: A chronic, noncontagious autoimmune condition where T-cells accelerate skin cell turnover from weeks to days, piling up immature keratinocytes into thick, well-demarcated red plaques covered with silvery scales (most common on elbows, knees, scalp, and sacrum). Approximately 30% of clients also develop psoriatic arthritis. Care: Apply prescribed topical corticosteroids, vitamin D analogues, and thick emollients; ensure exposure to controlled sunlight as directed; avoid stress and skin trauma (Koebner phenomenon). • Scabies: Infestation by the microscopic itch mite Sarcoptes scabiei, which burrows into the outer skin layer to lay eggs. Hallmark symptom is intense, unbearable itching that is noticeably worse at night, with tiny thread-like burrows and pimple-like rash between fingers, wrists, armpits, and waistline. Scabies is spread through skin-to-skin contact and shared bedding. Treatment involves full-body application of topical permethrin cream from neck to toes, left on for 8 to 14 hours before washing off. All household contacts must be treated simultaneously. Wash all bedding, towels, and clothing used in the prior 3 days in hot water and dry on high heat cycle.
• 银屑病（牛皮癣）：一种慢性、非传染性自身免疫性皮肤病。T淋巴细胞异常活化促使表皮角质形成细胞更新周期从正常的28天缩短至3-4天，表皮未成熟角化细胞在体表大量堆积，形成边界极其清楚、覆有厚层银白色云母状鳞屑的暗红斑块（好发于肘、膝、头皮及骶尾部）。约30%的患者合并银屑病性关节炎。护理：遵医嘱规律涂抹外用糖皮质激素、维生素D3衍生物及保湿软膏；指导适度接受医用紫外线光疗；避免精神压力、感染及皮肤外伤（防止诱发同形反应）。 • 疥疮（Scabies）：由人型疥螨寄生于人体表皮角质层挖掘隧道产卵引起的剧烈传染性寄生虫病。其绝对特征是夜间入睡后体温上升时奇痒难忍，在指缝、手腕屈侧、腋下及腰围等皮肤柔嫩处可见微小灰褐色线状隧道及丘疱疹。极易经皮肤直接接触或共用床单衣物迅速在家庭及护理院蔓延。治疗：在医生指导下自颈部以下全身涂抹5%二氯苯醚菊酯霜（百灭宁），保留8至14小时后彻底沐浴冲净。所有同住密接者必须同时用药治疗；前3天内使用过的全部床上用品、毛巾及贴身衣物必须立即用热水清洗并置于烘干机最高温档彻底烘干杀螨。
• Scleroderma (Systemic Sclerosis): A progressive autoimmune disorder characterized by excessive collagen accumulation causing severe hardening and tightening of the skin and subcutaneous connective tissue. In systemic scleroderma, the fibrotic process also attacks internal organs, including the esophagus, lungs, heart, and kidneys, leading to hypertension and organ failure. Fingers become stiff, clawed, and painful (sclerodactyly), with poor circulation (Raynaud's phenomenon). Care focuses on gentle skin moisturization, keeping extremities warm, pain relief, and empathetic support.
• 硬皮病（系统性硬化症）：一种进行性自身免疫性结缔组织病，因机体成纤维细胞过度活化分泌大量胶原纤维，导致皮肤与皮下组织发生广泛纤维化、僵硬增厚与弹性完全丧失。在系统型患者中，纤维化硬化会深入侵蚀食管黏膜、肺间质、心肌及肾脏微小动脉，引起恶性高血压及呼吸循环衰竭。患者双手手指常呈僵硬蜡白、屈曲挛缩似爪状（指硬化），并伴随严重的肢端遇冷苍白发绀缺血（雷诺现象）。日常照护核心在于温和保护脆弱变薄的皮肤、加强四肢保暖、缓解关节酸痛并给予深度的心理赋能陪伴。
PAGE 773 · 第 773 页 Shingles, Post-Herpetic Neuralgia, Skin Tags & Urticaria / 带状疱疹、带状疱疹后神经痛、皮赘与荨麻疹风团

SHINGLES (HERPES ZOSTER) AND POST-HERPETIC NEURALGIA

带状疱疹（缠腰龙）与顽固性带状疱疹后神经痛（PHN）
Shingles is a reactivation of the varicella-zoster virus (the virus that causes chickenpox). After a person recovers from childhood chickenpox, the virus retreats along sensory nerves and remains dormant in dorsal root ganglia. Decades later—triggered by aging, immunosuppression, chemotherapy, or severe stress—the virus reactivates, travels along a single sensory nerve path, and erupts on the skin. It presents as an intensely painful, burning, itching, unilateral band of vesicles (fluid-filled blisters) on one side of the body, most commonly encircling one side of the thorax or along facial branches of the trigeminal nerve. Post-Herpetic Neuralgia (PHN): In up to 20% of older clients, severe nerve pain (burning, stabbing, extreme sensitivity to light touch called allodynia) persists in the affected nerve pathway for months or years after the rash has completely healed. Shingles care involves antiviral medications started within 72 hours of rash onset, analgesic administration, cool compresses, loose non-constricting clothing, and strict protection against exposing individuals who have never had chickenpox (such as unvaccinated infants and pregnant women). The Shingrix vaccine provides over 90% protection against shingles in adults over age 50.
带状疱疹（Shingles / Herpes Zoster）是由潜伏体内的水痘-带状疱疹病毒被重新激活所致。初次感染水痘痊愈后，病毒并未离开人体，而是长期潜伏于脊髓后根神经节或颅神经感觉神经节内。数十年后，随着衰老机体细胞免疫力下降、恶性肿瘤放化疗或严重精神压力，潜伏病毒被再次激活，沿着单侧感觉神经纤维迅速逆行蔓延至体表，引发神经水肿与皮肤疱疹。其典型临床特征是：沿身体单侧某一神经节支配区呈带状排列、伴随剧烈神经烧灼痛、刀割样刺痛的群集性水疱，最常好发于单侧胸肋部或三叉神经第一支支配的头面部眼周。 带状疱疹后神经痛（PHN）：高达20%的高龄长者在疱疹皮损完全结痂脱落消退数月甚至数年后，受损神经分布区依然残留长期顽固性撕裂样、电击样神经剧痛（连最轻微的衣物触碰都能诱发剧烈疼痛，即触诱发痛 Allodynia）。照护要领：皮疹出现72小时内遵医嘱早期应用抗病毒药物；规律给予镇痛药支持；局部凉湿敷镇痛；穿着极其宽松柔软的棉质衣物以减少皮肤摩擦；严禁未患过水痘的易感人群（尤其是未接种疫苗的婴幼儿及孕妇）接触疱疹渗出液，防止其发生重症水痘。50岁以上中老年人接种重组带状疱疹疫苗（Shingrix）具有超过90%的卓越保护效力。

Skin Tags (Acrochordons) and Urticaria (Hives & Wheals)

皮赘（软纤维瘤）与荨麻疹（风团与红晕反应）
• Skin Tags (Acrochordons): Soft, small, benign, pedunculated (attached by a narrow stalk) outgrowths of skin. They are harmless and painless, commonly appearing in areas where skin rubs against skin or clothing, such as the neck, underarms, beneath breasts, and groins. They require no medical treatment unless they become irritated by friction or cosmetically concerning, in which case a physician can remove them via cryotherapy or surgical excision. • Urticaria (Hives) & Wheal Formation: Urticaria is a common hypersensitivity skin reaction caused by histamine release from cutaneous mast cells in response to allergens (such as shellfish, nuts, antibiotics, insect stings) or physical stimuli (cold, heat, pressure). It is clinically diagnosed by the eruption of wheals—raised, circular or irregularly shaped, spongy, intensely pruritic lesions that appear suddenly and are surrounded by an erythematous area of redness called a flare. Individual wheals typically resolve within minutes to hours, often fading in one area while new ones erupt elsewhere. Emergency Alert (Angioedema & Anaphylaxis): If hives are accompanied by swelling of the lips, tongue, or pharynx (angioedema), difficulty swallowing, or wheezing/dyspnea, the client is experiencing systemic anaphylaxis—a life-threatening medical emergency requiring immediate administration of epinephrine and emergency medical dispatch (911).
• 皮赘（Acrochordons / 软纤维瘤）：体表柔软、细小、完全良性的带蒂皮肤赘生物。通常完全无痛无害，好发于皮肤与皮肤或衣物反复摩擦的褶皱区域（颈周、腋下、乳房下缘及腹股沟）。除因经常摩擦红肿受损或出于美观需求由医生施行冷冻或激光剪切外，通常无需任何临床干预。 • 荨麻疹（Hives / Urticaria）与风团（Wheals）：荨麻疹是由机体肥大细胞脱颗粒释放大量组胺导致真皮微血管充血渗出所致的急性过敏性皮肤反应。诱发因素包括食物变应原（海鲜、坚果）、药物（青霉素类抗生素）、蚊虫叮咬或物理冷热刺激。其核心标志性皮损为「风团」（Wheals）——突发出现的扁平凸起、形态不规则或圆形、按压质软如海绵、伴随剧烈灼痒的水肿性斑块，周围常环绕一圈鲜红色的扩张血管红晕（Flare）。单个风团往往在数分钟至数小时内自行消退并不留任何痕迹，但其他部位又可此起彼伏此消彼长。 急救红线预警（血管神经性水肿与过敏性休克）：若荨麻疹发作时伴随口唇、舌体、面部或喉头声门深层水肿（血管神经性水肿 Angioedema）、吞咽困难或突发呼吸喘鸣、气促发绀，表明患者已陷入极度危险的全身过敏性休克（Anaphylaxis），必须立即呼叫急救医疗服务（911）并紧急使用肾上腺素自动注射笔（EpiPen）进行肌肉注射，分秒必争挽救生命。
PAGE 774 · 第 774 页 Musculoskeletal System: Fracture Types & Classification / 肌肉骨骼系统：骨折分类学与临床病理改变

MUSCULOSKELETAL DISORDERS: FRACTURES

肌肉骨骼系统疾病：骨折病理与分类
A fracture is a break or disruption in the continuity of a bone. Along with the structural break in the osseous tissue, surrounding soft tissues—including periosteum, skeletal muscles, blood vessels, peripheral nerves, and tendons—are inevitably stretched, contused, or lacerated. Fractures can occur from traumatic forces (falls, motor vehicle collisions, sports injuries, violence) or as pathological fragility fractures resulting from underlying disease (osteoporosis, metastatic bone cancer). Classification of Fractures: 1. Closed Fracture (Simple Fracture): The bone is broken cleanly beneath the surface, but the overlying skin remains intact. There is no external communication with the outside environment, greatly reducing the risk of osteomyelitis. 2. Open Fracture (Compound Fracture): The broken bone ends pierce through the overlying skin, or an external wound penetrates down to the bone. Open fractures are surgical emergencies because pathogens directly contaminate bone marrow, carrying an extreme risk of severe infection and osteomyelitis. 3. Spiral Fracture: The fracture line spirals around the long axis of the shaft, typically produced by powerful torsional (twisting) forces. In pediatric patients, unexplained spiral fractures of the femur or humerus are major clinical red flags for non-accidental physical abuse. 4. Greenstick Fracture: An incomplete fracture where one cortex of the bone breaks while the opposite cortex bends, resembling a snapped green twig. Because pediatric bones are more pliable, greenstick fractures occur almost exclusively in young children.
骨折（Fracture）是指骨的完整性或连续性发生部分或完全中断。骨质断裂的同时，骨折断端周围的软组织——包括骨膜、骨骼肌、毛细血管、外周神经束及肌腱韧带——不可避免地遭受严重牵拉、挫伤撕裂甚至神经离断。骨折可由直接或间接外力创伤（高处坠落、车祸撞击、运动外伤、暴力击打）引起，亦可在轻微外力甚至日常负重下因骨骼原发病变（重度骨质疏松症、原发性或转移性骨肿瘤）而发生病理性脆性骨折。 骨折的核心临床分类： 1. 闭合性骨折（Closed / Simple Fracture）：骨质发生断裂，但骨折断端上方的皮肤及黏膜保持完整无破损。骨折端不与外界相通，深部组织未遭外界细菌直接污染，发生化脓性感染和骨髓炎的风险相对较低。 2. 开放性骨折（Open / Compound Fracture）：骨折锐利断端刺破皮肤穿出体外，或外界致伤物穿透皮肤直达骨折端。开放性骨折属于骨科急诊，外界各种耐药菌与污染物直接进入髓腔，发生严重深部感染、坏疽及慢性化脓性骨髓炎的风险极高。 3. 螺旋形骨折（Spiral Fracture）：骨折线沿骨干长轴呈螺旋状延伸，通常由剧烈的扭转外力（Twisting force）所致。在儿科临床中，婴幼儿股骨或肱骨无法解释的螺旋形骨折是筛查非意外性躯体虐待（儿童虐待）的关键警示指征。 4. 青枝骨折（Greenstick Fracture）：骨质发生不完全断裂，一侧骨皮质断裂而另一侧骨皮质受压弯曲褶皱，如同折断新鲜柔嫩的绿树枝。由于儿童骨骼有机质含量高、骨膜肥厚坚韧，青枝骨折几乎绝大多数发生在婴幼儿及儿童群体中。

FIGURE 33.6: Types of bone fractures. A, Open (compound) fracture with bone piercing skin. B, Closed (simple) fracture with intact skin. C, Spiral fracture caused by twisting. D, Greenstick fracture common in children.

图 33.6：骨折四大常见类型。A：骨折端刺破皮肤的开放性（复合）骨折。B：皮肤完整的闭合性（单纯）骨折。C：扭转外力引起的螺旋形骨折。D：多见于儿童的青枝不完全性骨折。

PAGE 775 · 第 775 页 Fracture Reduction, Healing & Cast Care Guidelines / 骨折复位愈合机制与石膏固定临床护理规范

FRACTURE REDUCTION AND IMMOBILIZATION

骨折复位、生物学固定与愈合过程
The goal of fracture management is to restore the anatomical alignment of the broken bone fragments (reduction) and immobilize them until solid osseous union occurs, restoring maximal limb function. Reduction can be closed (manipulating bone fragments into position externally without incision, under regional or general anesthesia) or open (open reduction internal fixation / ORIF, where an orthopedic surgeon makes a surgical incision and secures fragments using surgical pins, plates, intramedullary rods, or screws). Following reduction, the limb is immobilized using casts, splints, or traction.
骨折治疗的三大根本原则是复位（Reduction）、固定（Immobilization）与功能锻炼（Rehabilitation），旨在将错位的骨折断端恢复到正常的解剖对应关系并使其稳定维持，直至坚固的骨小梁骨桥愈合，最大程度恢复肢体运动与负重功能。复位方式分为：闭合复位（在局部或全身麻醉下，由骨科医生通过手法牵引与推顶复位骨折端，无需切开皮肤）与切开复位内固定（ORIF——手术切开软组织直视下复位，并使用医用高强度金属接骨板、螺钉、髓内针或经皮克氏针进行刚性力学固定）。复位完成后，患肢必须使用石膏绷带、高分子夹板或持续牵引进行牢固外固定。

Casting and Professional Cast Care Guidelines

石膏固定与支持工作者关键护理操作红线
Plaster of Paris or synthetic fiberglass casts are molded around the injured limb. A wet plaster cast takes 24 to 48 hours to dry completely, while synthetic fiberglass sets within 15 to 30 minutes. Strict support worker care guidelines: • Palmar Support Rule: While a cast is wet or drying, handle it ONLY with the flat palms of your hands, never with your fingertips. Fingertip pressure indents the malleable cast material, creating permanent internal ridges that press on underlying skin and cause devastating pressure ulcers. • Air Exposure: Keep the wet cast uncovered and exposed to air.
